      /// Where the video is actually drawn inside the canvas.
      ///
      /// The layer letterboxes with `videoGravity = .resizeAspect`, so the picture occupies a
      /// centred rect whose aspect matches the *captured pixels*. Every touch has to be mapped
      /// through that rect, or the pointer lands proportionally wrong the moment the two aspects
      /// differ — which on a phone showing a 16:10 Mac display is always.
      enum CanvasGeometry {
          /// The layer's letterboxed rect for a captured size, inside `bounds`.
          static func videoRect(bounds: CGSize, pixelSize: CGSize?) -> CGRect {
              guard let pixelSize, pixelSize.width > 0, pixelSize.height > 0,
                    bounds.width > 0, bounds.height > 0
              else {
                  return CGRect(origin: .zero, size: bounds)
              }
              let scale = min(bounds.width / pixelSize.width, bounds.height / pixelSize.height)
              let size = CGSize(width: pixelSize.width * scale, height: pixelSize.height * scale)
              return CGRect(
                  x: (bounds.width - size.width) / 2,
                  y: (bounds.height - size.height) / 2,
                  width: size.width,
                  height: size.height
              )
          }
      
          /// Whether a touch belongs to the window's own chrome rather than to the Mac.
          ///
          /// The gesture surface is a UIKit view and the chrome is drawn over it by SwiftUI, which is
          /// not enough to keep a touch on a button from reaching the Mac as well. A pan recogniser
          /// begins after ten points of travel, and a thumb pressing a button near the edge of the
          /// screen slides further than that without meaning to — so the drag starts on the surface
          /// underneath and the Mac's pointer jumps to wherever the finger happened to be, while the
          /// button it was aimed at also fires. There is nothing in the geometry to tell the two apart;
          /// the caller knows where its chrome is, so it says so.
          static func isBlocked(_ point: CGPoint, regions: [CGRect]) -> Bool {
              regions.contains { $0.contains(point) }
          }
      
          /// Whether a tap should put something of ours away rather than reach the Mac.
          ///
          /// A panel and the software keyboard are the same thing from the picture's point of view:
          /// something of ours is open over it, and a tap on the picture belongs to closing that rather
          /// than to the Mac underneath. One decision asked twice with different answers for "is it
          /// open", rather than two functions that would drift apart at the first change.
          ///
          /// A pure decision for the same reason ``InteractionMode``'s routing is: a
          /// `UITapGestureRecognizer` cannot be driven from a unit test, and the whole of the behaviour
          /// is this one question. The chrome check is what keeps a tap *on* the panel from counting —
          /// a tap inside one belongs to the button under it, and dismissing on that would close the
          /// panel out from under the tile the user was aiming at.
          static func dismissesOverlay(at point: CGPoint, regions: [CGRect], isOpen: Bool) -> Bool {
              isOpen && !isBlocked(point, regions: regions)
          }
      
          /// Turns a touch into a normalized position, undoing the canvas's zoom and pan first.
          ///
          /// Normalized — never pixels. The host maps the result through its own transform, which
          /// is the only side that knows where the captured content sits in the Mac's global
          /// coordinate space, and which changes under it when a window moves or a display is
          /// switched. A client that sent pixels would be wrong the first time either happened.
          static func normalized(
              point: CGPoint,
              bounds: CGSize,
              pixelSize: CGSize?,
              zoom: CGFloat,
              pan: CGSize
          ) -> NormalizedPoint? {
              guard bounds.width > 0, bounds.height > 0, zoom > 0 else { return nil }
              let rect = videoRect(bounds: bounds, pixelSize: pixelSize)
              guard rect.width > 0, rect.height > 0 else { return nil }
      
              let centre = CGPoint(x: bounds.width / 2, y: bounds.height / 2)
              let content = CGPoint(
                  x: (point.x - centre.x - pan.width) / zoom + centre.x,
                  y: (point.y - centre.y - pan.height) / zoom + centre.y
              )
      
              let x = (content.x - rect.minX) / rect.width
              let y = (content.y - rect.minY) / rect.height
              // Clamped rather than rejected. A finger that slides off the edge of a letterboxed
              // picture has still expressed "as far as possible in that direction", and dropping
              // the sample would make the cursor stall at the boundary while the finger keeps
              // moving — which reads as a broken connection rather than as a limit.
              return NormalizedPoint(x: Double(min(max(x, 0), 1)), y: Double(min(max(y, 0), 1)))
          }
      }

          private func attachmentBool(_ sample: CMSampleBuffer, _ key: CFString) -> Bool? {
              CMGetAttachment(sample, key: key, attachmentModeOut: nil)
                  .map { CFBooleanGetValue($0 as! CFBoolean) }
          }
      
          // Gesture locations and the bounds used to normalize them must come from the same UIKit
          // view. RemoteScreen used to mix this full-screen coordinate space with its safe-area-sized
          // SwiftUI root, shifting the Mac cursor much farther in portrait than in landscape.
          @MainActor
          func testPortraitGestureSurfaceUsesItsOwnBoundsForCentreTap() async throws {
              let normalized = try await normalizedCentreReportedByGestureSurface(
                  size: CGSize(width: 430, height: 932)
              )
      
              XCTAssertEqual(normalized.x, 0.5, accuracy: 0.001)
              XCTAssertEqual(normalized.y, 0.5, accuracy: 0.001)
          }
      
          @MainActor
          func testLandscapeGestureSurfaceUsesItsOwnBoundsForCentreTap() async throws {
              let normalized = try await normalizedCentreReportedByGestureSurface(
                  size: CGSize(width: 932, height: 430)
              )
      
              XCTAssertEqual(normalized.x, 0.5, accuracy: 0.001)
              XCTAssertEqual(normalized.y, 0.5, accuracy: 0.001)
          }
      
          // MARK: - Interaction mode
      
          // The mode is the whole safety story of the in-session screen: drag & zoom is only worth
          // having if a finger in it cannot reach the Mac. These pin the routing rather than the
          // recognisers, because a `UIPanGestureRecognizer` cannot be driven from a unit test — and
          // "nothing was sent" is exactly the claim that has to keep holding.
      
          func testViewAndScrollTouchesReachTheMac() {
              let mode = InteractionMode.control
      
              XCTAssertFalse(mode.oneFingerDragPansCanvas)
              XCTAssertTrue(mode.tapsReachTheMac)
          }
      
          /// Looking closer is not a mode.
          ///
          /// The pinch used to be drag & zoom's alone, because a zoom silently rescaled the canvas and
          /// every click after it was mapped through that scale. `CanvasGeometry.normalized` undoes
          /// both the zoom and the pan now, so the pinch is free in both modes — and what the mode
          /// decides is only whether a *drag* pans, which it does once the picture overflows its view
          /// and there is somewhere to pan to.
          func testViewAndScrollZoomsAlwaysButPansOnlyWhenOverflowing() {
              let mode = InteractionMode.control
      
              XCTAssertTrue(mode.pinchZoomsCanvas)
              XCTAssertFalse(mode.twoFingerDragPansCanvas(isZoomed: false))
              XCTAssertTrue(mode.twoFingerDragPansCanvas(isZoomed: true))
          }
      
          func testDragAndZoomTouchesNeverReachTheMac() {
              let mode = InteractionMode.navigate
      
              XCTAssertTrue(mode.oneFingerDragPansCanvas)
              // Unconditional here: drag & zoom has no other meaning for the gesture to fall back to.
              XCTAssertTrue(mode.twoFingerDragPansCanvas(isZoomed: false))
              XCTAssertTrue(mode.twoFingerDragPansCanvas(isZoomed: true))
              XCTAssertTrue(mode.pinchZoomsCanvas)
              XCTAssertFalse(mode.tapsReachTheMac)
          }
      
          func testObserveModeCanInspectThePictureWithoutInput() {
              let mode = InteractionMode.observe
      
              XCTAssertTrue(mode.oneFingerDragPansCanvas)
              XCTAssertTrue(mode.twoFingerDragPansCanvas(isZoomed: false))
              XCTAssertTrue(mode.pinchZoomsCanvas)
              XCTAssertFalse(mode.tapsReachTheMac)
          }
      
          // MARK: - Putting a panel away
      
          // A panel is modal in intent but not in shape: it covers a corner of the picture and the rest
          // of the screen is still the Mac. These pin which taps put it away, for the same reason the
          // routing above is pinned rather than driven through a recogniser.
      
          private var panelRegion: CGRect { CGRect(x: 0, y: 700, width: 520, height: 120) }
      
          func testTapOnThePictureDismissesAnOpenPanel() {
              XCTAssertTrue(CanvasGeometry.dismissesOverlay(
                  at: CGPoint(x: 200, y: 300),
                  regions: [panelRegion],
                  isOpen: true
              ))
          }

          /// The enlarged pointer, which is a setting because of where it goes wrong.

          private var pointerRow: some View {
              HStack(spacing: 12) {
                  Image(systemName: "cursorarrow")
                      .font(.system(size: 16, weight: .medium))
                      .foregroundStyle(model.enlargePointer ? HostPalette.ink : HostPalette.muted)
                      .frame(width: 26, height: 26)
      
                  VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: 2) {
                      Text("Larger pointer")
                          .font(.subheadline.weight(.medium))
                      // Says what *off* means, because it is not "draw nothing" and it is not
                      // "no pointer": the system's pointer goes back into the capture and the small
                      // one the phone had before returns. The system pointer always remains in the
                      // capture because its movement is what keeps the video smooth on a quiet Mac.
                      Text("Draws a bigger pointer over the Mac's pointer in the video so it is easier to see on a phone. The pointer on this Mac is not changed. With this off only the Mac's smaller pointer is sent.")

                          .foregroundStyle(.secondary)

      import Foundation
      
      /// Keyboard modifiers, as an `OptionSet` over the platform's own bit layout.
      ///
      /// The raw values match `CGEventFlags` / `NSEvent.ModifierFlags`, so the host translates
      /// with a bare `CGEventFlags(rawValue:)` at the injection boundary and nowhere else.
      /// Verified against `NSEvent.h`: shift `1<<17`, control `1<<18`, option `1<<19`,
      /// command `1<<20`.
      ///
      /// Defined here rather than borrowed from AppKit because `NSEvent` does not exist in the
      /// iOS SDK — importing it would make this module macOS-only, which is precisely the trap
      /// that made the original spec's `RemoteCommand` uncompilable on the client.
      public struct Modifiers: OptionSet, Sendable, Hashable {
          public let rawValue: UInt
          public init(rawValue: UInt) { self.rawValue = rawValue }
      
          public static let shift   = Modifiers(rawValue: 1 << 17)
          public static let control = Modifiers(rawValue: 1 << 18)
          public static let option  = Modifiers(rawValue: 1 << 19)
          public static let command = Modifiers(rawValue: 1 << 20)
      
          public static let none: Modifiers = []
      }
      
      extension Modifiers: Codable {
          public init(from decoder: any Decoder) throws {
              let container = try decoder.singleValueContainer()
              self.init(rawValue: try container.decode(UInt.self))
          }
      
          public func encode(to encoder: any Encoder) throws {
              var container = encoder.singleValueContainer()
              try container.encode(rawValue)
          }
      }
      
      public enum MouseButton: UInt8, Codable, Sendable {
          case left = 0
          case right = 1
          case middle = 2
      }
      
      public enum SpaceDirection: String, Codable, Sendable {
          case left, right
      }
      
      /// How a queued command may be treated when a newer one arrives.
      ///
      /// The distinction matters and is easy to get wrong: coalescing a *relative* command by
      /// "newest wins" silently discards the earlier deltas, so a fast scroll gesture arrives
      /// as a single small nudge.
      public enum Coalescing: Sendable {
          /// Never drop or reorder. Press/release pairing is correctness: a dropped
          /// `mouseUp` leaves a button stuck down on the host.
          case none
          /// The value is an absolute position, so a newer sample supersedes an older one.
          case replaceLatest
          /// The value is a relative delta, so queued samples must be **summed**, not dropped.
          case accumulate
      }
      
      /// A single instruction for the host.
      ///
      /// Coordinates are normalized `0…1` rather than pixels, which is what makes them survive
      /// a zoomed client canvas and a mid-session display switch.
      public enum RemoteCommand: Codable, Sendable, Equatable {
          case mouseMove(to: NormalizedPoint)
          case mouseDown(button: MouseButton)
          case mouseUp(button: MouseButton)
          /// Relative scroll deltas, in the client's units. Accumulates.
          case scroll(dx: Double, dy: Double)
          /// Keycode mode: a real hardware keycode, so shortcuts like ⌘S and ⌘Tab behave
          /// exactly as they do on a physical keyboard.
          case keyDown(keyCode: UInt16, modifiers: Modifiers)
          case keyUp(keyCode: UInt16, modifiers: Modifiers)
          /// Unicode mode: text injected via `CGEventKeyboardSetUnicodeString`. Layout-independent,
          /// so emoji and CJK work, but shortcuts that listen for physical keycodes will not fire.
          case textInput(String)
          case switchSpace(direction: SpaceDirection)
          /// Jump directly to a 1-based Space index on one display. The host resolves it to a CGS
          /// workspace ID, so this works even if the user's Mission Control keyboard shortcuts are
          /// disabled.
          ///
          /// `display` is a `CGDirectDisplayID`'s raw value. Spaces are per-display, so an index on
          /// its own does not name a desktop: the same 1 is valid on every screen, and switching the
          /// wrong screen's first desktop is a wrong answer that looks like a right one.
          case switchToSpace(display: UInt32, index: Int)
          case joystickMove(dx: Double, dy: Double)
          /// Client-driven source switch. Applied with `SCStream.updateContentFilter(_:)`,
          /// so the stream is never torn down.
          case setCaptureSource(CaptureSource)
          /// Launches a locally installed GUI app. The host resolves the bundle identifier from
          /// its own application catalogue; clients never send executable paths.
          case launchApplication(bundleIdentifier: String)
          /// Requests a live change to the stream's quality. The host applies `bitrateKbps` to the
          /// running encoder and `fps` to the capture pipeline's frame interval. A value of 0 for
          /// either field means "keep current". Takes effect on the next keyframe.
          case setStreamQuality(fps: Int, bitrateKbps: Int)
          /// Asks the host to re-photograph the desktop being shown, ahead of the schedule it would
          /// otherwise keep to.
          ///
          /// A signal rather than a request for data: the picture arrives the way every other preview
          /// does, on the catalogue the host pushes when one lands. What this buys is *timing*. The
          /// desktops panel is the one moment a user can see how old the pictures are, and the host
          /// has no way to know it has been opened — this is the client saying so.
          case refreshSpacePreviews
          /// Dims supported physical displays to zero brightness while leaving capture running.
          /// `false` restores the levels saved when the mode was enabled.
          case setPrivacyMode(enabled: Bool)
          /// Locks the active macOS session. Unlocking remains a local action at the Mac.
          case lockScreen
          /// Asks the host to read its pasteboard and reply with a ``MessageKind/clipboardContent`` frame.
          case requestClipboard
          /// Pushes text from the phone's clipboard to the host pasteboard.
          case pushClipboard(text: String)
      
          public var coalescing: Coalescing {
              switch self {
              case .mouseMove:
                  return .replaceLatest
              case .joystickMove, .scroll:
                  return .accumulate
              case .mouseDown, .mouseUp, .keyDown, .keyUp, .textInput, .switchSpace,
                   .switchToSpace, .setCaptureSource, .launchApplication, .setStreamQuality,
                   .refreshSpacePreviews, .setPrivacyMode, .lockScreen,
                   .requestClipboard, .pushClipboard:
                  return .none
              }
          }
      
          /// Whether this command must survive a bounded send queue even when it is full.
          /// Anything whose loss would leave the host in a stuck state takes priority.
          public var isCritical: Bool {
              switch self {
              case .mouseDown, .mouseUp, .keyDown, .keyUp, .setCaptureSource, .launchApplication,
                   .setPrivacyMode, .lockScreen, .pushClipboard:
                  return true
              default:
                  return false
              }
          }
      
          /// Commands that can change what a person or application on the Mac is doing.
          /// Stream/session controls stay available in observe mode; pointer and keyboard input do not.
          public var isUserInput: Bool {
              switch self {
              case .mouseMove, .mouseDown, .mouseUp, .scroll, .keyDown, .keyUp, .textInput,
                   .switchSpace, .switchToSpace, .joystickMove:
                  return true
              case .setCaptureSource, .launchApplication, .setStreamQuality, .refreshSpacePreviews,
                   .setPrivacyMode, .lockScreen, .requestClipboard, .pushClipboard:
                  return false
              }
          }
      }
      import Foundation
      
      /// The type of a wire message, carried as the first byte of every frame.
      ///
      /// The first frame on a connection is always ``connectionRole``; see ``ConnectionRole``.
      public enum MessageKind: UInt8, Sendable, CaseIterable {
          /// Sent first on every connection to declare whether it carries control or video.
          case connectionRole = 0x01
      
          // Handshake (unauthenticated phase)
          case clientHello = 0x02
          case serverHello = 0x03
      
          // Pairing
          case pairConfirm = 0x04
          case pairResult = 0x05
          /// Hash commitment to an ephemeral public key, sent *before* any reveal.
          ///
          /// The order is the security property: see ``PairingSAS``. Without it an active
          /// attacker can grind a keypair until its own contribution produces the digits the
          /// real host is about to display, and the two screens agree by construction.
          case pairCommit = 0x0E
          case pairReveal = 0x0F
      
          // Control channel
          case command = 0x06
          case catalog = 0x07
          case setCaptureSource = 0x08
          case ping = 0x0B
          case pong = 0x0C
          case error = 0x0D
      
          // Video channel
          case videoConfig = 0x09
          case videoFrame = 0x0A
      
          /// A standing condition on the host that the client should say out loud.
          ///
          /// Distinct from ``error``, which ends the session. This is the middle case the protocol
          /// did not have: nothing is broken — the control channel is fine, the video channel is
          /// fine — but what the phone is looking at is not what it thinks. A sleeping display is
          /// the case this exists for: the stream is healthy, the frames are arriving, and they are
          /// all black, which from the phone is indistinguishable from a broken camera.
          ///
          /// Carries `nil` to clear.
          case notice = 0x10
      
          /// The host's clipboard, sent in response to a ``RemoteCommand/requestClipboard`` command
          /// or pushed proactively when it changes. Carries a ``ClipboardPayload``.
          case clipboardContent = 0x11
      
          /// Configuration for the audio stream that follows on the video connection.
          ///
          /// Sent once per stream start, ahead of the first ``audioFrame``. JSON-encoded
          /// ``AudioStreamConfig``, matching the convention used for ``videoConfig``.
          case audioConfig = 0x13
      
          /// One chunk of raw PCM audio. Fixed binary header + interleaved Float32 samples.
          /// Layout matches ``EncodedAudioFrame``.
          case audioFrame = 0x14
      }
      
      public enum FramingError: Error, Equatable, Sendable {
          /// The declared length exceeded ``FrameDecoder/maxFrameLength``. Thrown *before*
          /// any attempt to buffer the payload, so a hostile length field cannot exhaust memory.
          case frameTooLarge(declared: UInt32)
          /// A frame must carry at least the kind byte, so a length below 1 is malformed.
          case zeroLengthFrame
          /// The kind byte was not a known ``MessageKind``. Almost always means the peers are
          /// speaking different protocol versions; failing loudly beats silently skipping bytes.
          case unknownKind(UInt8)
      }
      
      /// A single decoded wire message.
      public struct Frame: Equatable, Sendable {
          public let kind: MessageKind
          public let payload: Data
      
          public init(kind: MessageKind, payload: Data = Data()) {
              self.kind = kind
              self.payload = payload
          }
      }
      
      /// Incremental decoder for the wire format:
      ///
      /// ```
      /// ┌──────────────┬─────────┬─────────────────────┐
      /// │ u32 length   │ u8 kind │ payload (length-1)  │
      /// │ big-endian   │         │                     │
      /// └──────────────┴─────────┴─────────────────────┘
      /// ```
      ///
      /// `length` counts the kind byte plus the payload.
      ///
      /// Feed it whatever bytes the transport hands over — it makes no assumptions about
      /// message boundaries, which is the whole point. TCP delivers an arbitrary byte window,
      /// so frames arrive split, coalesced, or several at a time.
      public struct FrameDecoder {
          /// Hard ceiling on a single frame. Enforced on the *declared* length before buffering,
          /// so a corrupt or hostile header is rejected instead of allocating gigabytes.
          public static let maxFrameLength: UInt32 = 16 * 1024 * 1024
      
          private var buffer: [UInt8] = []
          /// Start of the not-yet-consumed region. Avoids O(n) `removeFirst` per frame.
          private var readIndex = 0
      
          public init() {}
      
          /// Bytes received but not yet consumed. Exposed for tests and diagnostics.
          public var bufferedByteCount: Int { buffer.count - readIndex }
      
          public mutating func append<D: DataProtocol>(_ data: D) {
              buffer.append(contentsOf: data)
          }
      
          /// Returns the next complete frame, or `nil` if more bytes are needed.
          ///
          /// Call repeatedly until it returns `nil` — a single read may deliver several frames.
          /// Throws only on genuinely malformed input, after which the stream is unusable.
          public mutating func next() throws -> Frame? {
              guard bufferedByteCount >= 4 else {
                  compactIfWorthwhile()
                  return nil
              }
      
              let length = UInt32(buffer[readIndex]) << 24
                  | UInt32(buffer[readIndex + 1]) << 16
                  | UInt32(buffer[readIndex + 2]) << 8
                  | UInt32(buffer[readIndex + 3])
      
              guard length >= 1 else { throw FramingError.zeroLengthFrame }
              guard length <= Self.maxFrameLength else {
                  throw FramingError.frameTooLarge(declared: length)
              }
      
              let total = 4 + Int(length)
              guard bufferedByteCount >= total else {
                  compactIfWorthwhile()
                  return nil
              }
      
              let rawKind = buffer[readIndex + 4]
              guard let kind = MessageKind(rawValue: rawKind) else {
                  throw FramingError.unknownKind(rawKind)
              }
      
              let payload = Data(buffer[(readIndex + 5)..<(readIndex + total)])
              readIndex += total
              compactIfWorthwhile()
      import RemoteCore
      import SwiftUI
      import UIKit
      
      /// The root. Two screens, and which one is showing is a direct function of the session phase —
      /// there is no separate "am I connected" flag that could disagree with the phase.
      struct ContentView: View {
          @Environment(SessionModel.self) private var model
          @Environment(\.accessibilityReduceMotion) private var reduceMotion
      
          /// Whether the first-run introduction has been seen.
          ///
          /// The second question this router asks, and deliberately a separate one from the first.
          /// `phase` answers "is there a session"; this answers "has this person been here before" —
          /// and the two are unrelated, which is why they are two branches rather than one condition
          /// with an `&&` in it.
          @AppStorage(PreferenceKey.hasSeenWelcome) private var hasSeenWelcome = false
      
          private var isRemoteScreen: Bool {
              switch model.phase {
              case .ready, .confirming: return true
              default: return false
              }
          }
      
          /// How one screen hands over to another.
          ///
          /// Asymmetric, because the two are not equals: one is being left and the other arrived at.
          /// The outgoing screen recedes, shrinking away from the viewer, while the incoming one comes
          /// up through it slightly oversized and settles — which is the shape of moving *into*
          /// something rather than of one thing being swapped for another. Opacity and a small scale,
          /// deliberately nothing more: a slide would say the two screens sit side by side, and a blur
          /// would cost a frame of live video to buy nothing.
          private static let screenHandover = AnyTransition.asymmetric(
              insertion: .opacity.combined(with: .scale(scale: 1.06, anchor: .center)),
              removal: .opacity.combined(with: .scale(scale: 0.94, anchor: .center))
          )
      
          /// Whether the connecting card is up, and when the attempt started.
          ///
          /// Owned here rather than by the picker, and that is the whole of why it was never seen:
          /// the picker is *removed* the moment the session becomes ready, which is well inside the
          /// time the card needs to be legible — so a card drawn on it was built and destroyed with
          /// the screen it belonged to. This router outlives the swap, so the card can.
          /// The failure the picker's alert has already seen.
          ///
          /// Owned here rather than in `HostPickerView` so that `dismissConnecting()` can mark a
          /// failure shown on the ConnectingView as acknowledged before the picker becomes visible —
          /// otherwise the picker's alert fires for the same failure the moment the card fades away.
          @State private var acknowledgedFailure: String?
          @State private var showsConnecting = false
          @State private var connectingSince: Date?
          /// Why the attempt failed, once it has. Held here rather than left to the picker's alert:
          /// a failure that arrives while this card is up has to be said on the card, because the
          /// card is what the user is looking at.
          @State private var connectingFailure: String?
          /// Set when an attempt has run long enough that saying nothing would look like a hang.
          @State private var connectingIsSlow = false
          /// The successful handshake gets one legible beat before the veil lifts. This is separate
          /// from `showsConnecting`: removing the view and asking its transition to animate was easy
          /// for SwiftUI to fold into the simultaneous screen handover underneath it.
          @State private var connectingDidSucceed = false
          @State private var connectingIsFading = false
          @State private var slowTask: Task<Void, Never>?
          /// A successful connection may keep the card up briefly so it can be read. Stored so a
          /// failure arriving during that grace period can cancel the pending dismissal.
          @State private var connectingDismissTask: Task<Void, Never>?
      
          /// How long the connecting card stays up once a connection starts.
          ///
          /// Deliberately longer than the connection. A LAN handshake is over in a fraction of this,
          /// and the wait it is covering is real regardless — a TLS handshake and a certificate
          /// exchange with nothing to look at — so the card is given the time to be read as an
          /// arrival rather than a flicker. It is the one place in the app where waiting is the
          /// intended effect rather than a cost, and it is tuned here and nowhere else.
          private static let connectingShownFor: TimeInterval = 0.9
      
          /// How long a connection may take before the card stops pretending it is nearly there.
          ///
          /// A LAN connection is over in a fraction of a second and a relay one in a second or two, so
          /// this is well past anything normal — which is the point. It is not a timeout; nothing is
          /// cancelled at this mark. It is the moment the card stops saying "Connecting…" as though
          /// that were a description of something about to finish, and offers a way out instead.
          private static let connectingSlowAfter: TimeInterval = 12
      
          /// Long enough for the spinner-to-checkmark change to register, short enough that a slow
          /// connection does not make the user wait again after it has already finished.
          private static let connectingSuccessShownFor: TimeInterval = 0.28
      
          /// Exiting is a system response, so it is quicker and quieter than the modal's arrival.
          /// Opacity only: scaling the whole screen away makes the live desktop behind it appear to
          /// lurch forward at exactly the moment it should simply become available.
          private static let connectingFadeDuration: TimeInterval = 0.24
          private static let connectingFade = Animation.timingCurve(
              0.23, 1, 0.32, 1,
              duration: connectingFadeDuration
          )
      
          var body: some View {
              // A `ZStack`, not the `Group` this was, and the difference is the whole of the transition.
              //
              // A `Group` is transparent: its modifiers are applied to each branch separately rather
              // than to a container around them, so when the branch changes there is nothing that
              // survives the swap. No container whose frame could be interpolated, and never two views
              // on screen at once to carry an insertion and a removal together — the branch simply
              // changed. What that produces is a cross-fade, which is what a screen change looks like
              // when nobody designed it. A container has an identity that lasts through the change, so
              // both screens are briefly real and each can move on its own.
              ZStack {
                  // A session outranks the introduction, and the order is load-bearing rather than
                  // tidy. Two things connect without anyone tapping anything — the widget's
                  // `peering://connect` URL, and the automatic resume on a Mac coming back into range
                  // — so a first launch that was also a resume would otherwise land on a welcome
                  // screen with a live session behind it.
                  if isRemoteScreen {
                      RemoteScreen()
                          .transition(Self.screenHandover)
                  } else if !hasSeenWelcome {
                      WelcomeFlow { withAnimation(Motion.settle) { hasSeenWelcome = true } }
                          .transition(.opacity)
                  } else {
                      HostPickerView(acknowledgedFailure: $acknowledgedFailure)
                          .transition(Self.screenHandover)
                  }
      
                  // Last in the stack, so it covers whichever screen is underneath — including the
                  // moment the session replaces the picker. Nothing below it is reachable while it
                  // is up: a tap during a handshake that found the list would start a second
                  // connection for the same reason the first one was started.
                  if showsConnecting {
                      ConnectingView(
                          hostName: model.hostName,
                          failure: connectingFailure,
                          isSlow: connectingIsSlow,
                          isConnected: connectingDidSucceed,
                          onDismiss: dismissConnecting,
                          onCancel: cancelConnecting
                      )
                      // The exit is explicit rather than delegated to a removal transition. Keeping
                      // the full-screen veil mounted until opacity reaches zero makes the handoff
                      // deterministic even while the picker underneath becomes the remote screen.
                      .opacity(connectingIsFading ? 0 : 1)
                      .transition(
                          reduceMotion
                              ? .opacity
                              : .opacity.combined(with: .scale(scale: 1.04))
                      )
                  }
              }
              // Keyed to the connecting phases, not to `model.isBusy` — and that was the bug that made
              // this stick. `isBusy` answers "is a session live", which is true for `.ready` as well,
              // so a connection that *succeeded* never switched it off and the card stayed up over the
              // session it had just announced. The question this needs answered is narrower: is the
              // app in the part of a connection that has nothing to show yet.
              .onChange(of: isConnectingPhase, initial: true) { _, connecting in
                  connecting ? beginConnecting() : endConnecting()
              }
              // A failure that lands while the card is up belongs on the card, because the card is
              // what the user is looking at. Left to the picker's alert it would appear over the top
              // of this, having been written for a screen the user can no longer see.
              .onChange(of: model.phase) { _, phase in
                  guard case .failed(let message) = phase, showsConnecting else { return }
                  connectingDismissTask?.cancel()
                  connectingDismissTask = nil
                  withAnimation(reduceMotion ? .easeOut(duration: 0.16) : Motion.settle) {
                      connectingIsFading = false
                      connectingDidSucceed = false
                      connectingFailure = message
                  }
              }
              // Keyed to the arrival of a session rather than to `phase`, which changes several times
              // on the way in. Connecting and handshaking are the same screen still being looked at;
              // the change worth animating is the one where the picture takes over.
              .animation(Motion.settle, value: isRemoteScreen)
              .animation(Motion.settle, value: hasSeenWelcome)
              .onChange(of: isRemoteScreen, initial: true) { _, remote in
                  // The mask is what *permits* rotation, and it is read through
                  // `AppDelegate.application(_:supportedInterfaceOrientationsFor:)`.
                  OrientationLock.shared.allowed = remote ? .allButUpsideDown : .portrait
      
                  guard let scene = UIApplication.shared.connectedScenes
                      .compactMap({ $0 as? UIWindowScene }).first
                  else { return }
      
                  // Writing the mask is not enough, and this is the line that was missing.
                  // UIKit consults the delegate at its own discretion and otherwise keeps the
                  // value it last read — which was `.portrait`, set by the landing screen. So
                  // the remote screen went on offering portrait while `allowed` said otherwise,
                  // and no geometry request could fix that: the mask still excluded landscape.
                  scene.keyWindow?.rootViewController?.setNeedsUpdateOfSupportedInterfaceOrientations()
      
                  // Only the landing screen asks for an actual rotation. On the remote screen
                  // the device decides — requesting a geometry there would name the orientation
                  // the phone is already in (portrait, a member of the allowed set) and read as
                  // "stay put", which is what made rotation look disabled while sharing.
                  if !remote {
                      scene.requestGeometryUpdate(.iOS(interfaceOrientations: .portrait))
                  }
              }
          }
      
          /// Whether the app is in the part of a connection that has nothing to show yet.
          ///
          /// `.confirming` is deliberately excluded: by then there is a pairing code on screen and the
          /// session's own screen is the right place for it, so the card has already done its job.
          private var isConnectingPhase: Bool {
              switch model.phase {
              case .connecting, .handshaking: return true
              default: return false
              }

      import Foundation

      private let modelLog = Logger(subsystem: "pl.dmq.peering", category: "app")
      
      /// Which keyboard the user is driving the Mac with.
      ///
      /// The two modes are not a preference, they are different protocols on the wire, and the
      /// difference is user-visible:
      ///
      /// - ``unicode`` sends ``RemoteCommand/textInput(_:)``, which the host injects as Unicode
      ///   text. Layout-independent, so emoji, accented characters and CJK all arrive intact —
      ///   but a Mac shortcut that listens for a *physical* keycode (⌘S, ⌘Tab) never fires,
      ///   because no key was ever pressed.
      /// - ``keycode`` sends ``RemoteCommand/keyDown(keyCode:modifiers:)`` and ``keyUp`` with real
      ///   macOS virtual keycodes, so shortcuts behave exactly as they do on a hardware keyboard.
      ///   The cost is that a character with no keycode on the Mac's layout cannot be expressed.
      enum KeyboardMode: String, CaseIterable, Identifiable {
          case unicode
          case keycode
      
          var id: String { rawValue }
      
          var label: String {
              switch self {
              case .unicode: return "Unicode"
              case .keycode: return "Keycode"
              }
          }
      
          var explanation: String {
              switch self {
              case .unicode:
                  return "Sends text. Emoji and non-Latin scripts work; Mac shortcuts like ⌘S do not."
              case .keycode:
                  return "Sends real key presses with modifiers. Shortcuts work; characters outside the Mac's layout cannot be typed."
              }
          }
      }
      
      /// One macro button: a keycode with modifiers, sent as a press and a release.
      struct Macro: Identifiable, Hashable, Codable {
          let id: String
          let title: String
          let keyCode: UInt16
          let modifiers: Modifiers
      
          static let defaults: [Macro] = [
              // Keycodes are macOS virtual keycodes — the values `CGEvent` takes — not USB HID
              // usages and not characters. C is 8, V is 9, W is 13, Tab is 48, Space is 49.
              Macro(id: "copy",      title: "⌘C",     keyCode: 8,  modifiers: .command),
              Macro(id: "paste",     title: "⌘V",     keyCode: 9,  modifiers: .command),
              Macro(id: "cut",       title: "⌘X",     keyCode: 7,  modifiers: .command),
              Macro(id: "undo",      title: "⌘Z",     keyCode: 6,  modifiers: .command),
              Macro(id: "save",      title: "⌘S",     keyCode: 1,  modifiers: .command),
              Macro(id: "close",     title: "⌘W",     keyCode: 13, modifiers: .command),
              Macro(id: "appswitch", title: "⌘⇥",     keyCode: 48, modifiers: .command),
              Macro(id: "spotlight", title: "⌘Space", keyCode: 49, modifiers: .command),
              Macro(id: "escape",    title: "esc",    keyCode: 53, modifiers: []),
              Macro(id: "return",    title: "⏎",      keyCode: 36, modifiers: []),
              // Terminal / shell control sequences
              Macro(id: "ctrl-c",    title: "⌃C",     keyCode: 8,  modifiers: .control),
              Macro(id: "ctrl-z",    title: "⌃Z",     keyCode: 6,  modifiers: .control),
              Macro(id: "ctrl-d",    title: "⌃D",     keyCode: 2,  modifiers: .control),

              Macro(id: "ctrl-r",    title: "⌃R",     keyCode: 15, modifiers: .control),
          ]
      }

          // MARK: - Sleep prevention
      
          /// Toggles the lid-close sleep prevention assertion on or off.
          ///
          /// The assertion uses `PreventSystemSleep` — the same type `caffeinate -s` acquires —
          /// which survives closing the lid on most Macs. Unlike a `pmset` change it is
          /// process-scoped: the OS releases it automatically when this process exits, so a crash
          /// can never leave the Mac permanently unable to sleep.
          func setPreventLidCloseSleep(_ on: Bool) {
              preventLidCloseSleep = on
              UserDefaults.standard.set(on, forKey: "preventLidCloseSleep")
              if on {
                  acquireLidSleepAssertion()
              } else {
                  releaseLidSleepAssertion()
              }
          }
      
          private func acquireLidSleepAssertion() {
              guard lidSleepAssertionID == 0 else { return }
              let result = IOPMAssertionCreateWithName(
                  "PreventSystemSleep" as CFString,
                  IOPMAssertionLevel(kIOPMAssertionLevelOn),
                  "MacCast lid-close sleep prevention" as CFString,
                  &lidSleepAssertionID
              )
              if result != kIOReturnSuccess {
                  hostLog.notice("could not acquire lid sleep assertion (IOReturn \(result, privacy: .public))")
              }
          }
      
          private func releaseLidSleepAssertion() {
              guard lidSleepAssertionID != 0 else { return }
              IOPMAssertionRelease(lidSleepAssertionID)
              lidSleepAssertionID = 0
          }
      
          private func acquireSleepAssertion() {
              guard sleepAssertionID == 0 else { return }
              let reason = "MacCast session active" as CFString
              let result = IOPMAssertionCreateWithName(
                  kIOPMAssertionTypeNoDisplaySleep as CFString,
                  IOPMAssertionLevel(kIOPMAssertionLevelOn),
                  reason,
                  &sleepAssertionID
              )
              if result != kIOReturnSuccess {
                  hostLog.notice("could not acquire sleep assertion (IOReturn \(result, privacy: .public))")
              }
          }
      
          private func releaseSleepAssertion() {
              guard sleepAssertionID != 0 else { return }
              IOPMAssertionRelease(sleepAssertionID)
              sleepAssertionID = 0
          }
      
          // MARK: - Device name
      
          /// The name shown to a client in its device list.
          ///
          /// `Host.current().localizedName` is the "Computer Name" from Sharing settings —
          /// "Damian's MacBook Pro" — which is what the user actually recognises. The raw
          /// hostname (`damians-macbook-pro`) is only a fallback: it is what Bonjour would
          /// advertise by default, and a list of those is unreadable.
          private static var defaultDeviceName: String {
              if let localized = Host.current().localizedName, !localized.isEmpty {
                  return localized
              }
              let host = ProcessInfo.processInfo.hostName
              let suffix = ".local"
              return host.hasSuffix(suffix) ? String(host.dropLast(suffix.count)) : host
          }
      }

          func start() {

          private static let hostNamesKey = "Peering.hostNames"
      
          /// The failure to show the user, if the session is in one.
          ///
          /// The phase carries it and the diagnostics screen has always listed it, but the picker
          /// showed nothing at all — so a connection that failed instantly looked exactly like a
          /// button that did not work, which is a worse failure than the one being reported.
          var failureMessage: String? {
              if case .failed(let message) = phase { return message }
              return nil
          }
      
          /// The name to show for a trusted Mac, or nil when this device has never seen that ID —
          /// a Mac paired from somewhere else, or paired before names were being kept.
          /// Names the user has chosen, which win over the name the Mac reports.
          ///
          /// Kept apart from ``hostNames`` rather than overwriting an entry in it, and that is not
          /// tidiness: `hostNames` is rewritten from the Mac's own `ServerHello` on every connect, so a
          /// rename stored there would survive exactly until the next handshake and then quietly
          /// revert. Two maps, and the user's is asked first.
          ///
          /// Worth having for anyone whose Macs are all called the same thing — three MacBook Pros
          /// arrive as "Damian's MacBook Pro" three times over, and the device ID is the only thing
          /// telling them apart.
          private(set) var customHostNames: [String: String] = [:]
      
          private static let customHostNamesKey = "Peering.customHostNames"
      
          func displayName(forHostID id: String) -> String? { customHostNames[id] ?? hostNames[id] }
      
          /// Whether this Mac is showing a name the user chose rather than the one it reports.
          func isRenamed(hostID: String) -> Bool { customHostNames[hostID] != nil }
      
          /// Names a Mac, or puts its own name back if the new one is empty.
          func rename(hostID: String, to name: String) {
              let trimmed = name.trimmingCharacters(in: .whitespacesAndNewlines)
              if trimmed.isEmpty {
                  customHostNames.removeValue(forKey: hostID)
              } else {
                  customHostNames[hostID] = trimmed
              }
              UserDefaults.standard.set(customHostNames, forKey: Self.customHostNamesKey)
              publishKnownMacs()
          }

      import RemoteCore
      import SwiftUI
      import VideoToolbox
      
      /// Everything the session sheet used to hold, as a floating panel.
      ///
      /// A `List` of `Section`s does not survive being put on top of live video inside a sheet of glass —
      /// it brings its own background, its own separators and its own inset behaviour, all of which were
      /// chosen for a screen filling the display. This is the same content grouped by the panel's own
      /// rules, so the settings look like the rest of the in-session chrome rather than like a piece of
      /// iOS that landed on top of it.
      struct SettingsPanel: View {
          @Environment(SessionModel.self) private var model
      
          @Binding var chromeFrames: [ChromeRegion: CGRect]
      
          /// Opens the source picker in this same panel rather than stacking a second one beside it.
          var onShowSources: () -> Void
      
          @State private var confirmReset = false
          @State private var confirmLock = false
      
          /// Read here as well as in the screen that obeys it. Same key, one name for it in
          /// ``PreferenceKey`` — two spellings would be a switch that does nothing for one of them.
          @AppStorage(PreferenceKey.hidesPanelAfterSelect) private var hidesPanelAfterSelect = true
          /// Read here as well as in ``SessionPiP``, under the same key — see ``PreferenceKey``.
          @AppStorage(PreferenceKey.pipAutoStart) private var pipAutoStart = true
          /// Read here to say what it does in words, and in ``SessionModel`` to obey it. The default
          /// matches the model's: on, which is how the app behaved before the switch existed.
          @AppStorage(PreferenceKey.allowRelay) private var allowsRelay = true
      
          var body: some View {
              ScrollView(.vertical, showsIndicators: false) {
                  VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: 16) {
                      session
                      stream
                      picture
                      panels
                      privacy
                      keyboard
                      macros
                      pairedMacs
                      reset
                  }
              }
              .frame(maxWidth: .infinity, alignment: .leading)
              .padding(10)
              .glassEffect(.regular, in: RoundedRectangle(cornerRadius: 22, style: .continuous))
              .padding(.horizontal, 10)
              .reportChromeFrame(.panel, into: $chromeFrames)
              .transition(.move(edge: .bottom).combined(with: .opacity))
              .confirmationDialog(
                  "Reset this device's identity?",
                  isPresented: $confirmReset,
                  titleVisibility: .visible
              ) {
                  Button("Reset", role: .destructive) { model.resetDeviceIdentity() }
              } message: {
                  Text("Every Mac you have paired with will treat this device as a new one.")
              }
              .confirmationDialog(
                  "Lock this Mac?",
                  isPresented: $confirmLock,
                  titleVisibility: .visible
              ) {
                  Button("Lock Mac", role: .destructive) { model.lockMac() }
              } message: {
                  Text("The Mac will lock immediately. macOS does not allow a remote app to bypass its secure login screen, so someone must unlock it locally. Peering will reconnect automatically after wake or unlock.")
              }
          }
      
          private var session: some View {
              VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: 6) {
                  SectionLabel("Session")
                  info("Mac", model.hostName ?? "None")
                  info("State", stateDescription)
                  info("Path", model.connectionPath?.rawValue ?? "None")
                  info("Video", model.videoStatus)
                  if let latency = model.latencyMilliseconds {
                      info("Latency", "\(Int(latency.rounded())) ms")
                  }
                  info("This device", model.thisDeviceDescription)
                  Button(role: .destructive) { confirmLock = true } label: {
                      Label("Lock Mac · unlock locally", systemImage: "lock.fill")
                          .font(.subheadline.weight(.semibold))
                          .frame(maxWidth: .infinity)
                          .padding(.vertical, 9)
                          .rowSurface()
                          .contentShape(Rectangle())
                  }
                  .buttonStyle(RailButtonStyle(scale: 0.98))
              }
          }
      
          private var hevcSupported: Bool {
              VTIsHardwareDecodeSupported(kCMVideoCodecType_HEVC)
          }
      
          private var stream: some View {
              VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: 6) {
                  SectionLabel("Stream")
                  Button(action: onShowSources) {
                      HStack(spacing: 10) {
                          Image(systemName: "rectangle.on.rectangle")
                              .font(.system(size: 15, weight: .medium))
                              .foregroundStyle(ColorPalette.ink)
                          Text("Display, app or window")
                              .font(.subheadline.weight(.medium))
                          Spacer(minLength: 8)
                          Image(systemName: "chevron.right")
                              .font(.caption)
                              .foregroundStyle(.tertiary)
                      }
                      .padding(.horizontal, 10)
                      .padding(.vertical, 9)
                      .rowSurface()
                      .contentShape(Rectangle())
                  }
                  .buttonStyle(RailButtonStyle(scale: 0.98))
      
                  HStack(spacing: 10) {
                      Image(systemName: hevcSupported ? "checkmark.seal.fill" : "xmark.seal")
                          .font(.system(size: 15, weight: .medium))
                          .foregroundStyle(hevcSupported ? Color.green : Color.orange)
                      VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: 1) {
                          Text(hevcSupported ? "HEVC supported" : "HEVC not supported")
                              .font(.subheadline.weight(.medium))
                          Text(hevcSupported
                               ? "This device can decode H.265. Enable HEVC on the Mac for better quality at lower bandwidth."
                               : "This device cannot hardware-decode H.265. Use H.264 on the Mac for best performance.")
                              .font(.caption2)
                              .foregroundStyle(.secondary)
                              .fixedSize(horizontal: false, vertical: true)
                      }
                  }
                  .padding(.horizontal, 10)
                  .padding(.vertical, 9)
                  .rowSurface()
              }
          }
      
          private var panels: some View {
              VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: 6) {
                  SectionLabel("Panels")
                  Toggle(isOn: $hidesPanelAfterSelect) {
                      Text("Close after selecting")
                          .font(.subheadline.weight(.medium))
                  }
                  .tint(ColorPalette.accent)
                  Text("""
                      Desktops and sources put themselves away once you have picked one. Shortcuts \
                      stays open either way. Its buttons are key presses rather than choices, and a \
                      panel that shut after every one would have to be reopened for the next.
                      """)
                      .font(.caption2)
                      .foregroundStyle(.tertiary)
                      .fixedSize(horizontal: false, vertical: true)
              }
          }
      
          /// The floating window, and whether it arrives on its own.
          private var picture: some View {
              VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: 6) {
                  SectionLabel("Picture")
                  Toggle(isOn: $pipAutoStart) {
                      Text("Float automatically")
                          .font(.subheadline.weight(.medium))
                  }
                  .tint(ColorPalette.accent)
                  // Pushed straight through rather than read on the next attach: the setting has to
                  // take effect on the controller that is already running.
                  .onChange(of: pipAutoStart) { _, on in SessionPiP.shared.autoStart = on }
                  Text("Leaving the app floats the picture over whatever is next. The button in the status bar starts it by hand either way, and iOS has its own switch for this under Settings, General, Picture in Picture. Both have to allow it.")
                      .font(.caption2)
                      .foregroundStyle(.tertiary)
                      .fixedSize(horizontal: false, vertical: true)
              }
          }
      
          /// The one switch here that is about what leaves the device rather than how the app looks.
          ///
          /// Written out in full rather than left as a bare label, because a privacy setting the user
          /// cannot understand is a setting they cannot make. This is the switch; ``FAQView`` is the
          /// why, at length. Both have to say the same thing, and neither may say more than is true:
          /// the relay cannot read what it carries, and it does see who is connecting and when.
          private var privacy: some View {
              VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: 6) {
                  SectionLabel("Privacy")
                  Toggle(isOn: $allowsRelay) {
                      Text("Allow the relay")
                          .font(.subheadline.weight(.medium))
                  }
                  .tint(ColorPalette.accent)
                  Text("""
                      On: when your Mac is not on this network, Peering reaches it through a relay \
                      server. The connection is still end-to-end encrypted and the relay cannot read \
                      it, but the Mac's device ID and the times you connect do pass through that server.
      
                      Off: Peering only ever connects directly, over the network the two of you are \
                      both on. A Mac somewhere else is not reachable at all, and the app waits for it \
                      to come back rather than failing.
                      """)
                      .font(.caption2)
                      .foregroundStyle(.tertiary)
                      .fixedSize(horizontal: false, vertical: true)
                  Toggle(isOn: Binding(
                      get: { model.privacyModeEnabled },
                      set: { model.setPrivacyMode($0) }
                  )) {
                      Text("Darken the Mac's displays")
                          .font(.subheadline.weight(.medium))
                  }
                  .tint(ColorPalette.accent)
                  Text("Privacy Mode sets supported displays to 0% brightness without stopping the stream. Brightness is restored when you turn it off or disconnect. The Mac's physical brightness-up key always remains an escape hatch.")
                      .font(.caption2)
                      .foregroundStyle(.tertiary)
                      .fixedSize(horizontal: false, vertical: true)
              }
          }
      
          private var keyboard: some View {
              VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: 6) {
                  SectionLabel("Keyboard")
                  Picker("Mode", selection: Binding(
                      get: { model.keyboardMode },
                      set: { model.setKeyboardMode($0) }
                  )) {
                      ForEach(KeyboardMode.allCases) { mode in

                  }
                  .pickerStyle(.segmented)
                  Text(model.keyboardMode.explanation)
                      .font(.caption2)
                      .foregroundStyle(.secondary)
                      .fixedSize(horizontal: false, vertical: true)
              }
          }
      
          private var macros: some View {
              VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: 6) {
                  SectionLabel("Macros")
                  // Scrolls sideways rather than wrapping: the macros are a fixed, short list, and a
                  // wrapped grid would change the panel's height every time a title did.
                  ScrollView(.horizontal, showsIndicators: false) {
                      HStack(spacing: 6) {
                          ForEach(Macro.defaults) { macro in
                              Button { model.run(macro) } label: {
                                  Text(macro.title)
                                      .font(.caption.weight(.medium))
                                      .lineLimit(1)

                                      .rowSurface()

                      }
                      .padding(.vertical, 2)
                  }
              }
          }

                                  .font(.caption.weight(.semibold))
                                  .foregroundStyle(.secondary)
                                  .buttonStyle(RailButtonStyle())
                          }
                      }
                  }
              }
          }
      
          private var reset: some View {
              VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: 6) {
                  SectionLabel("This device")
                  HStack(spacing: 10) {
                      Image(systemName: "iphone")
                          .font(.system(size: 15, weight: .medium))
                          .foregroundStyle(ColorPalette.ink)
                      Text("Device name")
                          .font(.subheadline.weight(.medium))
                      Spacer(minLength: 8)
                      TextField("Name shown to Mac", text: Binding(
                          get: { model.device.name },
                          set: { model.setDeviceName($0) }
                      ))
                      .font(.subheadline)
                      .multilineTextAlignment(.trailing)
                      .foregroundStyle(.secondary)
                  }
                  .padding(.horizontal, 10)
                  .padding(.vertical, 9)
                  .rowSurface()
                  Text("The name Macs see for this device. iOS 16+ restricts apps from reading your custom device name without a special entitlement, so setting it here ensures Macs show the right name.")
                      .font(.caption2)
                      .foregroundStyle(.tertiary)
                      .fixedSize(horizontal: false, vertical: true)
      
                  Button(role: .destructive) { confirmReset = true } label: {

                          .font(.subheadline.weight(.medium))
                          .frame(maxWidth: .infinity)
                          .padding(.vertical, 9)
                          .rowSurface()
                          .contentShape(Rectangle())
                  }
                  .buttonStyle(RailButtonStyle(scale: 0.98))
                  Text("Generates a new key and a new device ID. Every Mac that knows this device must pair with it again.")
                      .font(.caption2)
                      .foregroundStyle(.tertiary)
                      .fixedSize(horizontal: false, vertical: true)

          }
      
          private func info(_ title: String, _ value: String) -> some View {
              HStack(spacing: 8) {
                  Text(title)
                      .font(.caption)
                      .foregroundStyle(.secondary)
                  Spacer(minLength: 8)
                  Text(value)
                      .font(.caption.weight(.medium))
                      .lineLimit(1)
                      .truncationMode(.middle)
              }
          }
      
          private var stateDescription: String {
              switch model.phase {
              case .idle: return "Idle"
              case .connecting: return "Connecting"
              case .handshaking: return "Handshaking"
              case .confirming: return "Waiting for the code comparison"
              case .ready: return "Connected"
              case .failed(let message): return "Failed: \(message)"
              case .disconnected: return model.isReconnecting ? "Offline · reconnecting…" : "Disconnected"
              }
          }
      }
