                  // and reading it from a closure that outlives this one is a data race the compiler
                  // is right to flag.
                  guard let self else { return }
                  // HostBrowser reports from its own queue. Every one of these touches observable
                  // state, so each is hopped deliberately rather than by luck.
                  Task { @MainActor in self.apply(event) }
              }
              browser.start()
          }
      
          func restartBrowsing() {
              browseStatus = "Looking for Macs on this network…"
              browser.stop()
              browser.start()
          }
      
          private func apply(_ event: HostBrowser.Event) {
              switch event {
              case .started:
                  browseFailed = false
                  browseStatus = "Looking for Macs on this network…"
              case .updated(let hosts):
                  browseFailed = false
                  self.hosts = hosts
                  if let pending = pendingHostID, let host = hosts.first(where: { $0.id == pending }) {
                      pendingHostID = nil
                      connect(to: .discovered(host))
                  }
                  self.resumeIfNeeded()
                  browseStatus = hosts.isEmpty
                      ? "No Macs found yet. Make sure Peering is running on your Mac and on the same network."
                      : "\(hosts.count) Mac\(hosts.count == 1 ? "" : "s") found"
              case .failed(let message):
                  // Bonjour failing is silent on iOS without the local-network permission and the
                  // NSBonjourServices entry, so the message is surfaced verbatim rather than
                  // smoothed over — the manual address path below is the way past it.
                  //
                  // Shown even though the browser is already replacing itself: the retry can take half
                  // a minute, and a status line that said nothing would leave the user looking at a
                  // list that has quietly stopped updating.
                  browseFailed = true
                  browseStatus = "Browsing failed: \(message)"
              }
          }
      
          // MARK: - Connecting
      
          /// Whether a session is up or on its way up. The same question as ``Phase/holdsSession`` —
          /// asked once, answered in one place.
          var isBusy: Bool { phase.holdsSession }
      
          func connect(to target: ClientSession.Target) {
              guard let identity, identityFailure == nil else { return }
      
              // Remembered for the home-screen widget, which can offer to connect but has no way of
              // knowing to what — see ``reconnectToLastHost()``. Written twice on purpose: the app
              // reads its own defaults, and the widget can only see the shared container.
              UserDefaults.standard.set(target.displayName, forKey: Self.lastHostNameKey)
              SharedStore.lastHostName = target.displayName
              switch target {
              case .discovered(let host):
                  connectionPath = .lan
                  UserDefaults.standard.set(host.id, forKey: Self.lastHostIDKey)
              case .relay(let hostID, _):
                  connectionPath = .relay
                  UserDefaults.standard.set(hostID, forKey: Self.lastHostIDKey)
              case .address:
                  connectionPath = .direct
                  // The handshake will replace this with the server's real ID. Keeping the ID of a
                  // different Mac during that window could make an automatic retry dial the relay for
                  // the wrong machine.
                  UserDefaults.standard.removeObject(forKey: Self.lastHostIDKey)
              }
              autoQualityPolicy = AutoQualityPolicy(path: autoQualityPath)
              lastAutoDroppedFrames = 0
              // A widget is not a live view and is not told anything. Without this it goes on showing
              // the previous Mac until the system next feels like refreshing it, which on a widget
              // that changes twice a year could be tomorrow.
              WidgetCenter.shared.reloadAllTimelines()
      
              // Intent does not survive into the next attempt: a flag left over from a session the
              // user ended would otherwise suppress the news that its successor has dropped.
              userEndedSession = false
              // An attempt from the backoff timer would race this one and start a second session.
              stopReconnecting()
              // And a session that is already up must be torn down rather than replaced. Assigning
              // over it leaves the first connection running with nothing holding it, so the peer sees
              // it drop mid-handshake — which reads on the Mac as a clean disconnect and, with a retry
              // following it, is a loop rather than a session.
              session?.disconnect()
              session = nil
              // The user has just asked to reach a Mac, which is the first moment the question
              // "tell you when it needs you?" means anything.
              notifier.prepareForFirstConnect()
      
              hostName = target.displayName
                          }
                      }
                  } else {
                      sessionStartedAt = nil
                  }
      
                  let userEnded = userEndedSession
                  userEndedSession = false
                  notifier.sessionMoved(
                      from: previous,
                      to: phase,
                      hostName: hostName ?? "the Mac",
                      deliberate: userEnded
                  )
                  // The same transition, said twice: once as something that can interrupt the user
                  // from another app, and once as something they will see without unlocking. Neither
                  // is a substitute for the other, and both read from this one place.
                  // A session that ended without the user asking is put back on its own. The flag
                  // inside `scheduleReconnect` is what decides whether this was theirs — the phase
                  // alone cannot tell a drop from a disconnect.
                  //
                  // Before the activity is told, not after: scheduling is what sets `isReconnecting`,
                  // and the card has to go up saying "reconnecting" rather than be taken down and
                  // brought back a moment later as though nothing had happened.
                  if !phase.holdsSession, !userEnded {
                      scheduleReconnect()
                  }
                  // Held open for as long as there is a session, so the socket outlives the app being
                  // on screen. Start before the card rather than after: the card is worth nothing if
                  // the session it describes has already been suspended. See ``SessionAudio``.
                  if phase.holdsSession {
                      SessionAudio.shared.begin()
                  } else {
                      SessionAudio.shared.end()
                  }
      
                  SessionActivity.shared.sessionMoved(
                      from: previous,
                      to: phase,
                      hostName: hostName ?? "the Mac",
                      startedAt: sessionStartedAt,
                      reconnecting: isReconnecting,
                      deliberate: userEnded,
                      path: Self.activityPath(connectionPath)
                  )
      
                  if !phase.holdsSession {
                      // The buttons stop claiming a modifier the moment the link that carried it is
                      // gone. Nothing else can: the release would have to travel back over the same
                      // connection. Observed on this Mac, the window server keeps the flag until a
                      // real key-up arrives, so the Mac may still be holding it — which is why this
                      // says so rather than quietly showing the buttons as released.
                      if !latchedModifiers.isEmpty {
                          append(note: "The connection ended while \(latchedModifiers.label) was held on the Mac. It may still be held there.")
                      }
                      latchedModifiers = []
                      privacyModeEnabled = false
                  }
              case .host(let hello):
                  // The host's own name is authoritative. Bonjour is only a hint about where to
                  // find it, and it can be stale by minutes.
                  hostName = hello.hostName
                  currentHostID = hello.serverID
                  UserDefaults.standard.set(hello.serverID, forKey: Self.lastHostIDKey)
                  if hostNames[hello.serverID] != hello.hostName {
                      hostNames[hello.serverID] = hello.hostName
                      UserDefaults.standard.set(hostNames, forKey: Self.hostNamesKey)
                      publishKnownMacs()
                  }
                  if let macs = hello.macAddresses, !macs.isEmpty {
                      hostMACAddresses[hello.serverID] = macs
                      if let data = try? JSONEncoder().encode(hostMACAddresses) {
                          UserDefaults.standard.set(data, forKey: "hostMACAddresses")
                      }
                  }
              case .catalog(let catalog):
                  self.catalog = catalog
                  if activeSourceID == nil, let first = catalog.displays.first ?? catalog.all.first {
                      activeSourceID = first.id
                  }
              case .video(let event):
                  apply(video: event)
              case .latency(let milliseconds):
                  latencyMilliseconds = milliseconds
              case .note(let message):
                  append(note: message)
              case .remoteNotice(let message):
                  remoteNotice = message
              case .clipboard(let text):
                  hostClipboard = text
                  clipboardPulling = false
              case .audio(let event):
                  switch event {
                  case .config(let config):
                      audioPlayer = RemoteAudioPlayer(config: config)
                  case .frame(let frame):
                      audioPlayer?.enqueue(frame)
                  }
              }
          }
      
          private func apply(video event: ClientSession.VideoEvent) {
              switch event {
              case .connecting:
                  videoStatus = "Opening the video channel…"
                  isVideoStreaming = false
              case .config(let config):
                  // The decoder half of this is done on the network queue, in the same place the
                  // frames are handled, so that it is ordered against them — see the event handler
                  // in `connect(to:)`. Flushing and reconfiguring here instead would put the reset
                  // *after* a keyframe that arrived in the same read, and throw that keyframe away.
                  // Only the labels belong on the main actor.
                  videoStatus = "\(config.codec.rawValue.uppercased()) \(config.pixelWidth)×\(config.pixelHeight)"
                  isVideoStreaming = true
                  append(note: "Video stream started: \(config.pixelWidth)×\(config.pixelHeight) \(config.codec.rawValue.uppercased())")
              case .frame:
                  break // Handled on the network queue; see `connect(to:)`.
              case .ended(let reason):
                  videoStatus = "Video ended: \(reason)"
                  isVideoStreaming = false
              }
          }
      
          private func startVideoPolling() {
              videoPoll?.cancel()
              videoPoll = Task { [weak self] in
                  while !Task.isCancelled {
                      try? await Task.sleep(for: .milliseconds(500))
                      guard let self else { return }
                      let snapshot = self.renderer.currentSnapshot
                      if snapshot.hasConfig {
                          self.updateFrameRate(from: snapshot)
                          self.updateAutoQuality(from: snapshot)
      
                          let suffix = snapshot.framesDropped > 0 ? " · \(snapshot.framesDropped) dropped" : ""
                          let waiting = snapshot.awaitingKeyframe ? " · waiting for a keyframe" : ""
                          // The *rate* leads, and the running total follows it. "1234 frames" answers
                          // how long the session has been up; the question a picture that looks choppy
                          // actually raises is how many are arriving each second, and only a
                          // difference between two samples can answer it.
                          let rate = self.videoFramesPerSecond > 0
                              ? " · \(Int(self.videoFramesPerSecond.rounded())) fps"
                              : ""
                          self.videoStatus = "\(snapshot.codec.rawValue.uppercased()) \(snapshot.pixelWidth)×\(snapshot.pixelHeight)\(rate) · \(snapshot.framesEnqueued) frames\(suffix)\(waiting)"
                      }
                  }
              }
          }
      
          /// Frames per second as they are actually arriving, smoothed.
          ///
          /// Derived from the difference between two polls rather than reported by anyone: neither end
          /// knows the rate at which the other is running, and the only place the two meet is here.
          /// Smoothed heavily because a half-second window is short enough that a single late batch
          /// reads as a swing, and what this is for is telling thirty apart from sixty.
          private func updateFrameRate(from snapshot: VideoRenderer.Snapshot) {
              let now = Date()
              defer { lastFrameSample = (snapshot.framesEnqueued, now) }
      
              guard let last = lastFrameSample else { return }
              // A count that has gone backwards is a reset, not a negative rate — the renderer drops
              // its totals when the stream restarts.
              guard snapshot.framesEnqueued >= last.count else {
                  videoFramesPerSecond = 0
                  return
              }
              let elapsed = now.timeIntervalSince(last.at)
              guard elapsed > 0 else { return }
      
              let rate = Double(snapshot.framesEnqueued - last.count) / elapsed
              videoFramesPerSecond = videoFramesPerSecond == 0
                  ? rate
                  : videoFramesPerSecond * 0.6 + rate * 0.4
          }
      
          private func append(note: String) {
              notes.append(note)
              if notes.count > 12 { notes.removeFirst(notes.count - 12) }
          }
      
          // MARK: - Pairing
      
          func confirmPairing(accepted: Bool) {
              // Declining ends the session from the Mac's side too, and that arrives as a
              // `.disconnected` like any other — with the model's phase still on `.confirming`.
              // Without this the user's own "no" comes back to them as a lost connection.
              if !accepted { userEndedSession = true }
              session?.confirmPairing(accepted: accepted)
          }
      
          /// Ends the session now.
          /// Ends the session now.
          ///
          /// `deliberate` answers "did the user mean to do this", which is not the same question as
          /// "who called this function". A disconnect the user tapped is silent; one iOS forced
          /// because it is about to suspend the app is not, because they are looking at something else
          /// and a banner is the only way they will ever hear about it.
          func disconnect(deliberate: Bool = true) {
              // Taken before anything else is, because everything below is about taking this session
              // apart — and the card that outlives a non-deliberate end needs both to say what was
              // lost. After the lines beneath them they are simply nil.
              let wasStartedAt = sessionStartedAt
              let wasPath = Self.activityPath(connectionPath)
      
              // Recorded before anything moves. `ClientSession` announces the end itself, and by
              // the time that event is applied the phase below has already been set — so this flag
              // is the only evidence left that the user, and not the network, ended it.
              userEndedSession = deliberate
              // Only a deliberate one. A drop, and the end iOS forces on a backgrounded app, are the
              // two cases where coming back to the app should find the session restored.
              if deliberate {
                  UserDefaults.standard.set(false, forKey: Self.resumeKey)
                  stopReconnecting()
              }
              sessionStartedAt = nil
              session?.disconnect()
              session = nil
              videoPoll?.cancel()
              videoPoll = nil
              renderer.reset()
              connectionPath = nil
              latchedModifiers = []
              phase = .disconnected
      
              // Below the assignment rather than above it: this is the path that *is* the phase
              // change, and the announcement that follows it sees no difference to act on.
              //
              // Which of the two, though, turns on `deliberate` — and this method is how *both*
              // arrive, because the end iOS forces when the background grace runs out comes through
              // here too. `sessionEnded()` used to be called unconditionally, so backgrounding the
              // app made the card vanish as though the user had quit: the one moment the card is
              // most useful, and it was being taken away. That is the opposite of what `deliberate`
              // is documented to mean two paragraphs up.
              // The keepalive and the floating window go with the session, and this method has to do
              // it itself for the same reason the card does: it sets the phase rather than letting
              // the event path announce it, so nothing that hangs off a phase *change* runs. Without
              // this, quitting left silence playing and a floating window of a Mac nobody is
              // connected to.
              SessionAudio.shared.end()
              SessionPiP.shared.stop()
      
              if deliberate {
                  SessionActivity.shared.sessionEnded()
              } else {
                  SessionActivity.shared.sessionDropped(
                      hostName: hostName ?? "the Mac",
                      startedAt: wasStartedAt,
          /// A value in `-1...1` cycling with `period` seconds, shifted `phase` of the way along.
          ///
          /// A sine rather than a triangle so the turnaround is soft at both ends; `easeInOut` was
          /// standing in for exactly this shape.
          private static func wave(_ now: TimeInterval, period: Double, phase: Double = 0) -> CGFloat {
              CGFloat(sin(2 * .pi * (now / period + phase)))
          }
      }
      
      
      /// Everything that is useful when something has gone wrong and noise otherwise.
      struct DiagnosticsView: View {
          @Environment(SessionModel.self) private var model
      
          var body: some View {
              List {
                  Section("Connection") {
                      LabeledContent("Mac", value: model.hostName ?? "None")
                      LabeledContent("State", value: state)
                      LabeledContent("Path", value: model.connectionPath?.rawValue ?? "None")
                      LabeledContent("Display", value: model.videoStatus)
                      if let tls = model.tlsDescription {
                          LabeledContent("Transport", value: tls)
                      }
                      if let latency = model.latencyMilliseconds {
                          LabeledContent("Control latency", value: "\(Int(latency.rounded())) ms")
                      }
                      LabeledContent("Source", value: model.activeSourceID ?? "None")
                  }
      
                  Section("This device") {
                      Text(model.thisDeviceDescription)
                          .font(.footnote.monospaced())
                  }
      
                  Section("Log") {
                      if model.notes.isEmpty {
                          Text("Nothing yet").foregroundStyle(.secondary)
                      } else {
                          ForEach(Array(model.notes.enumerated()), id: \.offset) { _, note in
                              Text(note).font(.caption)
                          }
                      }
                  }
              }
              .navigationTitle("Diagnostics")
          }
      
          private var state: String {
              switch model.phase {
              case .idle: return "Idle"
              case .connecting: return "Connecting"
              case .handshaking: return "Handshaking"
              case .confirming: return "Confirming the pairing code"
              case .ready: return "Connected"
              case .failed(let message): return "Failed: \(message)"
              case .disconnected: return "Disconnected"
              }
          }
      }

## Assistant

The Mac redesign and LAN/relay guidance are now in place, and both scoped builds passed. The cursor path is also changed end-to-end: the Mac no longer bakes its cursor into video, while the iPhone draws the commanded position immediately and uses the exact inverse of touch mapping so zoom/pan/letterboxing do not introduce drift. I’m now validating the two riskier pieces—the power assertion and audio transport—before folding in terminal sessions.

          // MARK: - Event handling
      
          private func apply(_ event: HostEvent) {
              switch event {
              case .listening(let port):
                  status = .listening(port: port)
                  record("Listening on port \(port) as “\(deviceName)”")
      
              case .stopped:
                  status = .idle
                  record("Stopped listening")
      
              case .clientConnected(let clientID, let clientName, let detail):
                  if !isConnected(clientID) {
                      connectedClients.append(
                          ConnectedClient(id: clientID, name: clientName, since: Date())
                      )
                  }
                  // Recorded before anything is shown, so the name outlives the connection that
                  // carried it. A client may rename itself between sessions, which is why this is
                  // assigned rather than only inserted.
                  if clientNames[clientID] != clientName {
                      clientNames[clientID] = clientName
                      UserDefaults.standard.set(clientNames, forKey: Self.clientNamesKey)
                  }
                  beginSession(id: clientID, name: clientName)
                  startScreenWatch()
                  record("\(clientName) connected · \(detail)")
                  // A client that has just arrived has no source list, so it is sent one now
                  // rather than waiting for an application to launch. It is also the first read of
                  // the catalogue, which is what needs Screen Recording permission — a refusal
                  // here is logged and otherwise ignored, and the video path reports it properly
                  // if the client then asks for a source.
                  coordinator?.refreshCatalog()
                  acquireSleepAssertion()
      
              case .clientDisconnected(let clientID, let clientName):
                  connectedClients.removeAll { $0.id == clientID }
                  endSession(id: clientID)
                  // The last one out stops the watch. Anybody still attached keeps being told.
                  if connectedClients.isEmpty { stopScreenWatch() }
                  record("\(clientName) disconnected")
                  // The moment nothing is left that can release what this client was holding.
                  // A client that dies mid-chord or mid-drag takes its key-up with it, and the
                  // Mac would otherwise sit holding ⌘ or a mouse button until a real one arrived
                  // from the physical keyboard — see ``InputInjector/releaseAll()``, which is
                  // where the reasoning lives. Only on the *last* client: the held state is the
                  // Mac's, so releasing it while another client is still connected would drop a
                  // modifier that client is holding for real.
                  if connectedClients.isEmpty {
                      input.releaseAll()
                      releaseSleepAssertion()
                      setPrivacyMode(false)
                  }
      
              case .pairingCode(let code, let clientName):
                  pairing = PairingPrompt(clientName: clientName, code: code)
                  record("Pairing code for \(clientName): \(code)")
      
              case .pairingCompleted(let clientName, let accepted):
                  pairing = nil
                  lastOutcome = PairingOutcome(clientName: clientName, accepted: accepted)
                  pairedClientIDs = pins?.peerIDs ?? []
                  record(accepted ? "Paired with \(clientName)" : "Pairing with \(clientName) rejected")
      
              case .failed(let message):
                  record(message)
                  // A failure while listening is worth surfacing as status; a per-connection
                  // failure is not, or one bad client would blank out a healthy listener.
                  if !status.isListening {
                  return false
              }
          }

          // still dim, but the CPU and network stay live so the session survives a dark screen.
          private var sleepAssertionID: IOPMAssertionID = 0
      
          // Optional stronger assertion that also prevents lid-close sleep.

          // automatically released when this process exits (even on crash).

          /// Whether a given trusted device is attached right now.
          ///
          /// Matched on the ID, which is the only thing the two lists have in common. The name is the
          /// client's to choose, two devices can present the same one, and comparing it against a list
          /// of IDs is a question that is always false — which is how a connected iPhone came to read
          /// as merely "Trusted".
          func isConnected(_ id: String) -> Bool {
              connectedClients.contains { $0.id == id }
          }
          /// Everything the host has said, newest last.
          ///
          /// Kept as entries rather than as formatted lines so the window can show when each thing
          /// happened and which of them went wrong, and can hand the whole list over in one copy.
          private(set) var log: [HostLogEntry] = []
      
      import Foundation
      
      /// One line in the host window's log.
      ///
      /// A struct rather than a formatted string, because the window needs three things a string has
      /// already thrown away: when it happened, whether it was bad, and the ability to be copied —
      /// the first two to show, the third to hand to someone else.
      struct HostLogEntry: Identifiable, Sendable, Equatable {
          /// How much attention a line deserves.
          ///
          /// Three cases and no more. A log with a severity ladder people have to learn is a log
          /// nobody reads; what matters at a glance is whether this is routine, surprising, or
          /// something that did not work.
          enum Level: Sendable, Equatable {
              case info
              case warning
              case failure
          }
      
          let id: UUID
          let at: Date
          let level: Level
          let message: String
      
          init(at: Date = Date(), level: Level = .info, message: String) {
              self.id = UUID()
              self.at = at
              self.level = level
              self.message = message
          }
      
          /// `14:32:07`, to the second.
          ///
          /// Seconds rather than minutes: the lines this log exists for are about ordering — which
          /// happened first when a session dropped — and a minute stamp cannot answer that.
          var time: String {
              Self.formatter.string(from: at)
          }
      
          /// Short language used by the visible activity feed. The copied log keeps the longer
          /// fixed-width words below because those are easier to scan in plain text.
          var levelName: String {
              switch level {
              case .info: return "Event"
              case .warning: return "Notice"
              case .failure: return "Failed"
              }
          }
      
          var levelSymbol: String {
              switch level {
              case .info: return "circle.fill"
              case .warning: return "exclamationmark.circle.fill"
              case .failure: return "xmark.octagon.fill"
              }
          }
      
          private static let formatter: DateFormatter = {
              let formatter = DateFormatter()
              formatter.dateFormat = "HH:mm:ss"
              return formatter
          }()
      }
      
      extension Array where Element == HostLogEntry {
          /// The whole log as plain text, for the pasteboard.
          ///
          /// Timestamps and a level word on each line, so a copy pasted into a message still says
          /// when each thing happened and which ones went wrong — which is the entire reason for
          /// copying it.
          var plainText: String {
              map { entry in
                  let level = switch entry.level {
                  case .info: "     "
                  case .warning: "WARN "
                  case .failure: "FAIL "
                  }
                  return "\(entry.time)  \(level)\(entry.message)"
              }
              .joined(separator: "\n")
          }
      }

      # Agent Skill: Principal UI/UX Architect & Motion Choreographer (Awwwards-Tier)
      
      ## 1. Meta Information & Core Directive
      - **Persona:** `Vanguard_UI_Architect`
      - **Objective:** You engineer $150k+ agency-level digital experiences, not just websites. Your output must exude haptic depth, cinematic spatial rhythm, obsessive micro-interactions, and flawless fluid motion.
      - **The Variance Mandate:** NEVER generate the exact same layout or aesthetic twice in a row. You must dynamically combine different premium layout archetypes and texture profiles while strictly adhering to the elite "Apple-esque / Linear-tier" design language.
      
      ## 2. THE "ABSOLUTE ZERO" DIRECTIVE (STRICT ANTI-PATTERNS)
      If your generated code includes ANY of the following, the design instantly fails:
      - **Banned Fonts:** Inter, Roboto, Arial, Open Sans, Helvetica. (Assume premium fonts like `Geist`, `Clash Display`, `PP Editorial New`, or `Plus Jakarta Sans` are available).
      - **Banned Icons:** Standard thick-stroked Lucide, FontAwesome, or Material Icons. Use only ultra-light, precise lines (e.g., Phosphor Light, Remix Line).
      - **Banned Borders & Shadows:** Generic 1px solid gray borders. Harsh, dark drop shadows (`shadow-md`, `rgba(0,0,0,0.3)`).
      - **Banned Layouts:** Edge-to-edge sticky navbars glued to the top. Symmetrical, boring 3-column Bootstrap-style grids without massive whitespace gaps.
      - **Banned Motion:** Standard `linear` or `ease-in-out` transitions. Instant state changes without interpolation.
      
      ## 3. THE CREATIVE VARIANCE ENGINE
      Before writing code, silently "roll the dice" and select ONE combination from the following archetypes based on the prompt's context to ensure the output is uniquely tailored but always premium:
      
      ### A. Vibe & Texture Archetypes (Pick 1)
      1. **Ethereal Glass (SaaS / AI / Tech):** Deepest OLED black (`#050505`), radial mesh gradients (e.g., subtle glowing purple/emerald orbs) in the background. Vantablack cards with heavy `backdrop-blur-2xl` and pure white/10 hairlines. Wide geometric Grotesk typography.
      2. **Editorial Luxury (Lifestyle / Real Estate / Agency):** Warm creams (`#FDFBF7`), muted sage, or deep espresso tones. High-contrast Variable Serif fonts for massive headings. Subtle CSS noise/film-grain overlay (`opacity-[0.03]`) for a physical paper feel.
      3. **Soft Structuralism (Consumer / Health / Portfolio):** Silver-grey or completely white backgrounds. Massive bold Grotesk typography. Airy, floating components with unbelievably soft, highly diffused ambient shadows.
      
      ### B. Layout Archetypes (Pick 1)
      1. **The Asymmetrical Bento:** A masonry-like CSS Grid of varying card sizes (e.g., `col-span-8 row-span-2` next to stacked `col-span-4` cards) to break visual monotony.
         - **Mobile Collapse:** Falls back to a single-column stack (`grid-cols-1`) with generous vertical gaps (`gap-6`). All `col-span` overrides reset to `col-span-1`.
      2. **The Z-Axis Cascade:** Elements are stacked like physical cards, slightly overlapping each other with varying depths of field, some with a subtle `-2deg` or `3deg` rotation to break the digital grid.
         - **Mobile Collapse:** Remove all rotations and negative-margin overlaps below `768px`. Stack vertically with standard spacing. Overlapping elements cause touch-target conflicts on mobile.
      3. **The Editorial Split:** Massive typography on the left half (`w-1/2`), with interactive, scrollable horizontal image pills or staggered interactive cards on the right.
         - **Mobile Collapse:** Converts to a full-width vertical stack (`w-full`). Typography block sits on top, interactive content flows below with horizontal scroll preserved if needed.
      
      **Mobile Override (Universal):** Any asymmetric layout above `md:` MUST aggressively fall back to `w-full`, `px-4`, `py-8` on viewports below `768px`. Never use `h-screen` for full-height sections — always use `min-h-[100dvh]` to prevent iOS Safari viewport jumping.
      
      ## 4. HAPTIC MICRO-AESTHETICS (COMPONENT MASTERY)
      
      ### A. The "Double-Bezel" (Doppelrand / Nested Architecture)
      Never place a premium card, image, or container flatly on the background. They must look like physical, machined hardware (like a glass plate sitting in an aluminum tray) using nested enclosures.
      - **Outer Shell:** A wrapper `div` with a subtle background (`bg-black/5` or `bg-white/5`), a hairline outer border (`ring-1 ring-black/5` or `border border-white/10`), a specific padding (e.g., `p-1.5` or `p-2`), and a large outer radius (`rounded-[2rem]`).
      - **Inner Core:** The actual content container inside the shell. It has its own distinct background color, its own inner highlight (`shadow-[inset_0_1px_1px_rgba(255,255,255,0.15)]`), and a mathematically calculated smaller radius (e.g., `rounded-[calc(2rem-0.375rem)]`) for concentric curves.
      
      ### B. Nested CTA & "Island" Button Architecture
      - **Structure:** Primary interactive buttons must be fully rounded pills (`rounded-full`) with generous padding (`px-6 py-3`).
      - **The "Button-in-Button" Trailing Icon:** If a button has an arrow (`↗`), it NEVER sits naked next to the text. It must be nested inside its own distinct circular wrapper (e.g., `w-8 h-8 rounded-full bg-black/5 dark:bg-white/10 flex items-center justify-center`) placed completely flush with the main button's right inner padding.
      
      ### C. Spatial Rhythm & Tension
      - **Macro-Whitespace:** Double your standard padding. Use `py-24` to `py-40` for sections. Allow the design to breathe heavily.
      - **Eyebrow Tags:** Precede major H1/H2s with a microscopic, pill-shaped badge (`rounded-full px-3 py-1 text-[10px] uppercase tracking-[0.2em] font-medium`).
      
      ## 5. MOTION CHOREOGRAPHY (FLUID DYNAMICS)
      Never use default transitions. All motion must simulate real-world mass and spring physics. Use custom cubic-beziers (e.g., `transition-all duration-700 ease-[cubic-bezier(0.32,0.72,0,1)]`).
      
      ### A. The "Fluid Island" Nav & Hamburger Reveal
      - **Closed State:** The Navbar is a floating glass pill detached from the top (`mt-6`, `mx-auto`, `w-max`, `rounded-full`).
      - **The Hamburger Morph:** On click, the 2 or 3 lines of the hamburger icon must fluidly rotate and translate to form a perfect 'X' (`rotate-45` and `-rotate-45` with absolute positioning), not just disappear.
      - **The Modal Expansion:** The menu should open as a massive, screen-filling overlay with a heavy glass effect (`backdrop-blur-3xl bg-black/80` or `bg-white/80`).
      - **Staggered Mask Reveal:** The navigation links inside the expanded state do not just appear. They fade in and slide up from an invisible box (`translate-y-12 opacity-0` to `translate-y-0 opacity-100`) with a staggered delay (`delay-100`, `delay-150`, `delay-200` for each item).
      
      ### B. Magnetic Button Hover Physics
      - Use the `group` utility. On hover, do not just change the background color.
      - Scale the entire button down slightly (`active:scale-[0.98]`) to simulate physical pressing.
      - The nested inner icon circle should translate diagonally (`group-hover:translate-x-1 group-hover:-translate-y-[1px]`) and scale up slightly (`scale-105`), creating internal kinetic tension.
      
      ### C. Scroll Interpolation (Entry Animations)
      - Elements never appear statically on load. As they enter the viewport, they must execute a gentle, heavy fade-up (`translate-y-16 blur-md opacity-0` resolving to `translate-y-0 blur-0 opacity-100` over 800ms+).
      - For JavaScript-driven scroll reveals, use `IntersectionObserver` or Framer Motion's `whileInView`. Never use `window.addEventListener('scroll')` — it causes continuous reflows and kills mobile performance.
      
      ## 6. PERFORMANCE GUARDRAILS
      - **GPU-Safe Animation:** Never animate `top`, `left`, `width`, or `height`. Animate exclusively via `transform` and `opacity`. Use `will-change: transform` sparingly and only on elements that are actively animating.
      - **Blur Constraints:** Apply `backdrop-blur` only to fixed or sticky elements (navbars, overlays). Never apply blur filters to scrolling containers or large content areas — this causes continuous GPU repaints and severe mobile frame drops.
      - **Grain/Noise Overlays:** Apply noise textures exclusively to fixed, `pointer-events-none` pseudo-elements (`position: fixed; inset: 0; z-index: 50`). Never attach them to scrolling containers.
      - **Z-Index Discipline:** Do not use arbitrary `z-50` or `z-[9999]`. Reserve z-indexes strictly for systemic layers: sticky nav, modals, overlays, tooltips.
      
      ## 7. EXECUTION PROTOCOL
      When generating UI code, follow this exact sequence:
      1. **[SILENT THOUGHT]** Roll the Variance Engine (Section 3). Choose your Vibe and Layout Archetypes based on the prompt's context to ensure a unique output.
      2. **[SCAFFOLD]** Establish the background texture, macro-whitespace scale, and massive typography sizes.
      3. **[ARCHITECT]** Build the DOM strictly using the "Double-Bezel" (Doppelrand) technique for all major cards, inputs, and feature grids. Use exaggerated squircle radii (`rounded-[2rem]`).
      4. **[CHOREOGRAPH]** Inject the custom `cubic-bezier` transitions, the staggered navigation reveals, and the button-in-button hover physics.
      5. **[OUTPUT]** Deliver flawless, pixel-perfect React/Tailwind/HTML code. Do not include basic, generic fallbacks.
      
      ## 8. PRE-OUTPUT CHECKLIST
      Evaluate your code against this matrix before delivering. This is the last filter.
      - [ ] No banned fonts, icons, borders, shadows, layouts, or motion patterns from Section 2 are present
      - [ ] A Vibe Archetype and Layout Archetype from Section 3 were consciously selected and applied

      - [ ] CTA buttons use the Button-in-Button trailing icon pattern where applicable
      - [ ] Section padding is at minimum `py-24` — the layout breathes heavily
      - [ ] All transitions use custom cubic-bezier curves — no `linear` or `ease-in-out`
      - [ ] Scroll entry animations are present — no element appears statically
      - [ ] Layout collapses gracefully below `768px` to single-column with `w-full` and `px-4`
      - [ ] All animations use only `transform` and `opacity` — no layout-triggering properties
      - [ ] `backdrop-blur` is only applied to fixed/sticky elements, never to scrolling content
      - [ ] The overall impression reads as "$150k agency build", not "template with nice fonts"

      import Foundation
      
      /// A device's persistent, human-recognisable identity, as advertised over Bonjour.
      ///
      /// The ID is generated once and kept in `UserDefaults`, so the device is recognised as the
      /// same one across launches, network changes, and DHCP reassignments. Bonjour only carries
      /// it — its real job is to be stored by the peer next to the pin, so each side can tell
      /// "my Mac" from "some other Mac that happens to be on this café's Wi-Fi".
      ///
      /// It is deliberately *not* a secret and deliberately not derived from hardware. It is a
      /// name, and the pin is what actually authenticates.
      public struct DeviceIdentity: Sendable, Equatable {
          public let id: String
          public let name: String
      
          private static let idKey = "Peering.deviceID"
          private static let nameKey = "Peering.deviceName"
      
          public init(id: String, name: String) {
              self.id = id
              self.name = name
          }
      
          /// Loads the stored identity, minting a fresh ID on first run.
          ///
          /// `defaultName` is supplied by the caller rather than derived here: the sensible
          /// fallback differs by platform (`ProcessInfo.hostName` on macOS, `UIDevice.name` on
          /// iOS), and neither is available to a module that compiles for both.
          public static func loadOrCreate(
              defaultName: String,
              from defaults: UserDefaults = .standard
          ) -> DeviceIdentity {
              let id: String
              if let stored = defaults.string(forKey: idKey), !stored.isEmpty {
                  id = stored
              } else {
                  id = UUID().uuidString
                  defaults.set(id, forKey: idKey)
              }
      
              let storedName = defaults.string(forKey: nameKey)
              let name = (storedName?.isEmpty == false ? storedName : nil) ?? defaultName
              return DeviceIdentity(id: id, name: name)
          }
      
          /// Persists a user-chosen name.
          public func saveName(_ newName: String, to defaults: UserDefaults = .standard) {
              defaults.set(newName, forKey: Self.nameKey)
          }
      }
      import Foundation
      import Network
      
      /// Sends an IEEE 802 Wake-on-LAN magic packet to a single hardware MAC address.
      ///
      /// The packet is a 6-byte broadcast header (0xFF×6) followed by the target MAC repeated
      /// 16 times — 102 bytes total. Sent as a UDP broadcast on port 9 (the discard port, which
      /// is what routers expect) and also port 7 as a fallback.
      enum WakeOnLAN {
          /// Parses a colon-separated MAC address string and sends the magic packet.
          /// Silently does nothing on a malformed address.
          static func send(to macString: String) {
              let fields = macString.split(separator: ":", omittingEmptySubsequences: false)
              guard fields.count == 6,
                    fields.allSatisfy({ $0.count == 2 }),
                    fields.allSatisfy({ $0.allSatisfy(\.isHexDigit) })
              else { return }
              send(mac: fields.compactMap { UInt8($0, radix: 16) })
          }
      
          private static func send(mac: [UInt8]) {
              var packet = Data(repeating: 0xFF, count: 6)
              for _ in 0..<16 { packet.append(contentsOf: mac) }
              sendUDP(packet, port: 9)
              sendUDP(packet, port: 7)
          }
      
          private static func sendUDP(_ data: Data, port: UInt16) {
              let connection = NWConnection(
                  host: .ipv4(.broadcast),
                  port: NWEndpoint.Port(rawValue: port)!,
                  using: .udp
              )
              connection.stateUpdateHandler = { state in
                  switch state {
                  case .ready:
                      connection.send(content: data, completion: .contentProcessed { _ in
                          connection.stateUpdateHandler = nil
                          connection.cancel()
                      })
                  case .failed:
                      connection.stateUpdateHandler = nil
                      connection.cancel()
                  case .cancelled:
                      connection.stateUpdateHandler = nil
                  default:
                      break
                  }
              }
              connection.start(queue: .global(qos: .utility))
          }
      }
      import AVFoundation
      import Foundation
      import RemoteCore
      
      /// Renders incoming PCM audio frames from a Mac session.
      ///
      /// Built once per audio stream (one ``AudioStreamConfig`` → one player), so a stream
      /// restart — codec change, sample rate change — starts a fresh instance. The old one
      /// is simply released; AVAudioEngine cleans up on deinit.
      final class RemoteAudioPlayer: @unchecked Sendable {
          private let engine = AVAudioEngine()
          private let playerNode = AVAudioPlayerNode()
          private let format: AVAudioFormat
          private let channelCount: Int
      
          init?(config: AudioStreamConfig) {
              guard config.codec == .pcmFloat32,
                    config.sampleRate.isFinite,
                    (8_000...384_000).contains(config.sampleRate),
                    (1...32).contains(config.channelCount),
                    let fmt = AVAudioFormat(
                        commonFormat: .pcmFormatFloat32,
                        sampleRate: config.sampleRate,
                        channels: AVAudioChannelCount(config.channelCount),
                        interleaved: false
                    )
              else { return nil }
              self.format = fmt
              self.channelCount = config.channelCount
      
              engine.attach(playerNode)
              engine.connect(playerNode, to: engine.mainMixerNode, format: fmt)
              engine.mainMixerNode.outputVolume = 1.0
      
              do {
                  // Allow other audio sessions (music, calls) to continue alongside.
                  try AVAudioSession.sharedInstance().setCategory(.playback, mode: .default, options: .mixWithOthers)
                  try AVAudioSession.sharedInstance().setActive(true)
                  try engine.start()
                  playerNode.play()
              } catch {
                  return nil
              }
          }
      
          deinit {
              playerNode.stop()
              engine.stop()
          }
      
          /// Converts interleaved Float32 samples to a non-interleaved AVAudioPCMBuffer and schedules
          /// them for playback.
          func enqueue(_ frame: EncodedAudioFrame) {
              let (sampleValues, sampleOverflow) = frame.sampleCount.multipliedReportingOverflow(by: channelCount)
              let (expectedBytes, byteOverflow) = sampleValues.multipliedReportingOverflow(by: MemoryLayout<Float>.size)
              guard frame.channelCount == channelCount,
                    frame.sampleCount > 0,
                    !sampleOverflow,
                    !byteOverflow,
                    frame.samples.count == expectedBytes
              else { return }
              let frameCount = AVAudioFrameCount(frame.sampleCount)
              guard let buffer = AVAudioPCMBuffer(pcmFormat: format, frameCapacity: frameCount)
              else { return }
              buffer.frameLength = frameCount
      
              // De-interleave: input is L0 R0 L1 R1 …, AVAudioPCMBuffer expects channel planes.
              frame.samples.withUnsafeBytes { raw in
                  let floats = raw.bindMemory(to: Float.self)
                  for ch in 0..<channelCount {
                      guard let plane = buffer.floatChannelData?[ch] else { continue }
                      for i in 0..<Int(frameCount) {
                          let idx = i * channelCount + ch
                          plane[i] = idx < floats.count ? floats[idx] : 0
                      }
                  }
              }
      
              playerNode.scheduleBuffer(buffer, completionHandler: nil)
          }
      }

## User

the dont sleep screen switch doesnt work

## Assistant

Confirmed—the switch was only creating a `PreventSystemSleep` assertion, which keeps the Mac’s CPU/network awake but does not stop the display from sleeping. That is why it looked like the switch did nothing, and no permission prompt is expected for this API.

I’ve changed the switch to acquire both system-sleep and display-sleep assertions immediately. I’ll rebuild and verify the new binary with macOS’s own `pmset -g assertions` output before handing it back. Closed-lid behavior will still be subject to macOS clamshell/power rules; the app can request wakefulness but cannot bypass hardware lid policy.

    agent tool: Wait · Completed

      import SwiftUI
      
      /// The host's palette, matching the client's.
      ///
      /// Semantic rather than literal, which is the one place the two deliberately differ: the phone
      /// is dark by fiat, and this is a Mac app living in a window that may be in either appearance.
      /// `primary` and `secondary` *are* the ink and the muted ink of whichever one is current, so a
      /// state means the same thing in both and nothing has to be picked per mode.
      ///
      /// Two copies of eight constants, because sharing them would mean moving this into `RemoteCore`
      /// — the wire protocol — and a UI dependency there would outlive any benefit from saving them.
      enum HostPalette {
          /// The ink: selection, a live session, anything that is on.
          static let ink = Color.primary
          /// Muted ink: a state that is neither good nor bad, and the resting tone for labels.
          static let muted = Color.secondary
          /// The quietest tone that still reads: idle, absent, switched off.
          ///
          /// `Color.secondary` dimmed rather than a `.tertiary` style: there is no tertiary *colour*,
          /// only a hierarchical style, and this has to be usable anywhere a `Color` is expected.
          static let faint = Color.secondary.opacity(0.55)
          /// A panel's own fill.
          static let surface = Color(nsColor: .controlBackgroundColor)
          /// A fill that stands slightly off the panel behind it.
          static let raised = Color.primary.opacity(0.07)
      
          /// The cool tint behind detail panes. It is deliberately faint so native macOS materials
          /// still determine the actual light/dark appearance.
          static let canvasTint = Color.accentColor.opacity(0.045)
          /// Hairline used on elevated surfaces. A white/black adaptive line reads more naturally
          /// than a fixed grey in both appearances.
          static let hairline = Color.primary.opacity(0.09)
          /// State colours are reserved for operational feedback, never decoration.
          static let success = Color(nsColor: .systemGreen)
          static let caution = Color(nsColor: .systemOrange)
          static let danger = Color(nsColor: .systemRed)
      }
      
      enum HostMotion {
          static let hover = Animation.spring(response: 0.24, dampingFraction: 0.86)
          static let press = Animation.spring(response: 0.18, dampingFraction: 0.78)
          static let state = Animation.spring(response: 0.36, dampingFraction: 0.88)
      }
      
      extension View {
          /// A native-feeling elevated panel with just enough edge definition to survive both
          /// graphite light mode and the darkest macOS appearance.
          func hostPanel(cornerRadius: CGFloat = 14) -> some View {
              background(.regularMaterial, in: RoundedRectangle(cornerRadius: cornerRadius, style: .continuous))
                  .overlay {
                      RoundedRectangle(cornerRadius: cornerRadius, style: .continuous)
                          .strokeBorder(HostPalette.hairline)
                  }
                  .shadow(color: Color.black.opacity(0.06), radius: 14, y: 5)
          }
      
          /// The double bezel is reserved for the live stream: one crisp inner edge around the
          /// content and a softer outer rim make it read as the primary instrument in the window.
          func hostPrimaryPanel(cornerRadius: CGFloat = 18) -> some View {
              padding(4)
                  .background(.ultraThinMaterial, in: RoundedRectangle(cornerRadius: cornerRadius + 4, style: .continuous))
                  .overlay {
                      RoundedRectangle(cornerRadius: cornerRadius + 4, style: .continuous)
                          .strokeBorder(Color.white.opacity(0.16))
                  }
                  .overlay {
                      RoundedRectangle(cornerRadius: cornerRadius, style: .continuous)
                          .strokeBorder(HostPalette.hairline)
                          .padding(4)
                  }
                  .shadow(color: Color.accentColor.opacity(0.08), radius: 24, y: 9)
                  .shadow(color: Color.black.opacity(0.08), radius: 10, y: 4)
          }
      }
      
      struct HostHostingButtonStyle: ButtonStyle {
          let isHosting: Bool
      
          func makeBody(configuration: Configuration) -> some View {
              configuration.label
                  .foregroundStyle(isHosting ? Color.primary : Color.white)
                  .background(
                      isHosting ? HostPalette.raised : Color.accentColor,
                      in: RoundedRectangle(cornerRadius: 9, style: .continuous)
                  )
                  .overlay {
                      RoundedRectangle(cornerRadius: 9, style: .continuous)
                          .strokeBorder(isHosting ? HostPalette.hairline : Color.white.opacity(0.18))
                  }
                  .scaleEffect(configuration.isPressed ? 0.975 : 1)
                  .opacity(configuration.isPressed ? 0.82 : 1)
                  .shadow(
                      color: isHosting ? .clear : Color.accentColor.opacity(configuration.isPressed ? 0.08 : 0.2),
                      radius: configuration.isPressed ? 3 : 9,
                      y: configuration.isPressed ? 1 : 4
                  )
                  .animation(HostMotion.press, value: configuration.isPressed)
          }
      }

      import Foundation
      
      /// A terminal owned by the Mac host. The identifier remains stable while the control client
      /// disconnects, allowing a later authenticated connection to reattach to the same process.
      public struct TerminalSessionInfo: Codable, Equatable, Sendable, Identifiable {
          public let id: UUID
          public let name: String
          public let isRunning: Bool
      
          public init(id: UUID, name: String, isRunning: Bool) {
              self.id = id
              self.name = name
              self.isRunning = isRunning
          }
      }
      
      /// Requests sent by an authenticated client over the control connection.
      public enum TerminalRequest: Codable, Equatable, Sendable {
          case list
          case create(name: String)
          case input(sessionID: UUID, data: Data)
          case resize(sessionID: UUID, columns: UInt16, rows: UInt16)
          case close(sessionID: UUID)
      }
      
      public enum TerminalAttentionKind: String, Codable, Equatable, Sendable {
          case confirmation
          case password
      }
      
      /// Terminal state and byte output sent by the host. Output remains bytes because a PTY may
      /// split UTF-8 scalars between reads and emits ANSI control sequences by design.
      public enum TerminalEvent: Codable, Equatable, Sendable {
          case snapshot([TerminalSessionInfo])
          case created(TerminalSessionInfo)
          case output(sessionID: UUID, data: Data)
          case attention(sessionID: UUID, kind: TerminalAttentionKind, message: String)
          case exited(sessionID: UUID, status: Int32)
          case closed(sessionID: UUID)
          case failed(sessionID: UUID?, message: String)
      }

      import Darwin
      import Foundation
      import RemoteCore
      import os
      
      private let terminalLog = Logger(subsystem: "pl.dmq.peering.host", category: "terminal")
      
      /// Owns login shells independently of network connections. A disconnected phone can list the
      /// sessions later and receives the bounded output history before live output resumes.
      final class TerminalSessionManager: @unchecked Sendable {
          typealias Sink = @Sendable (TerminalEvent) -> Void
      
          private final class Session: @unchecked Sendable {
              let id: UUID
              let name: String
              let process: Process
              let master: FileHandle
              var isRunning = true
              var history = Data()
              var promptTail = ""
              var lastAttention: (kind: TerminalAttentionKind, time: Date)?
      
              init(id: UUID, name: String, process: Process, master: FileHandle) {
                  self.id = id
                  self.name = name
                  self.process = process
                  self.master = master
              }
      
              var info: TerminalSessionInfo {
                  TerminalSessionInfo(id: id, name: name, isRunning: isRunning)
              }
          }
      
          private let lock = NSLock()
          private var sessions: [UUID: Session] = [:]
          private var sinks: [String: Sink] = [:]
          private static let maximumHistoryBytes = 512 * 1024
      
          deinit {
              let active = lock.withLock { Array(sessions.values) }
              for session in active where session.isRunning { session.process.terminate() }
          }
      
          func handle(clientID: String, request: TerminalRequest, sink: @escaping Sink) {
              guard !clientID.isEmpty else {
                  sink(.failed(sessionID: nil, message: "The authenticated device has no identity."))
                  return
              }
              lock.withLock { sinks[clientID] = sink }
      
              switch request {
              case .list:
                  let snapshot = lock.withLock { sessions.values.map(\.info).sorted { $0.name < $1.name } }
                  sink(.snapshot(snapshot))
                  for info in snapshot {
                      let history = lock.withLock { sessions[info.id]?.history ?? Data() }
                      if !history.isEmpty { sink(.output(sessionID: info.id, data: history)) }
                  }
      
              case .create(let requestedName):
                  create(name: requestedName, sink: sink)
      
              case .input(let id, let data):
                  guard data.count <= 64 * 1024 else {
                      sink(.failed(sessionID: id, message: "Terminal input was too large."))
                      return
                  }
                  guard let session = lock.withLock({ sessions[id] }), session.isRunning else {
                      sink(.failed(sessionID: id, message: "That terminal is no longer running."))
                      return
                  }
                  do { try session.master.write(contentsOf: data) }
                  catch { sink(.failed(sessionID: id, message: "Could not write to the terminal.")) }
      
              case .resize(let id, let columns, let rows):
                  guard columns > 0, rows > 0,
                        let session = lock.withLock({ sessions[id] }) else { return }
                  var size = winsize(ws_row: rows, ws_col: columns, ws_xpixel: 0, ws_ypixel: 0)
                  if ioctl(session.master.fileDescriptor, TIOCSWINSZ, &size) == -1 {
                      terminalLog.error("could not resize terminal (id.uuidString, privacy: .public)")
                  }
      
              case .close(let id):
                  close(id: id)
              }
          }
      
          func disconnect(clientID: String) {
              lock.withLock { sinks.removeValue(forKey: clientID) }
          }
      
          private func create(name requestedName: String, sink: @escaping Sink) {
              let name = normalizedName(requestedName)
              let id = UUID()
              var masterFD: Int32 = -1
              var slaveFD: Int32 = -1
              var size = winsize(ws_row: 30, ws_col: 100, ws_xpixel: 0, ws_ypixel: 0)
              guard openpty(&masterFD, &slaveFD, nil, nil, &size) == 0 else {
                  sink(.failed(sessionID: nil, message: "Could not create a terminal."))
                  return
              }
      
              let master = FileHandle(fileDescriptor: masterFD, closeOnDealloc: true)
              let slave = FileHandle(fileDescriptor: slaveFD, closeOnDealloc: true)
              let process = Process()
              process.executableURL = URL(fileURLWithPath: loginShell())
              process.arguments = ["-l"]
              process.currentDirectoryURL = FileManager.default.homeDirectoryForCurrentUser
              var environment = ProcessInfo.processInfo.environment
              environment["TERM"] = "xterm-256color"
              environment["COLORTERM"] = "truecolor"
              process.environment = environment
              process.standardInput = slave
              process.standardOutput = slave
              process.standardError = slave
      
              let session = Session(id: id, name: name, process: process, master: master)
              process.terminationHandler = { [weak self, weak session] process in
                  guard let self, let session else { return }
                  self.didExit(session, status: process.terminationStatus)
              }
              master.readabilityHandler = { [weak self, weak session] handle in
                  guard let self, let session else { return }
                  let data = handle.availableData
                  if data.isEmpty { handle.readabilityHandler = nil; return }
                  self.received(data, from: session)
              }
      
              do {
                  try process.run()
                  lock.withLock { sessions[id] = session }
                  sink(.created(session.info))
                  terminalLog.notice("started terminal (name, privacy: .public) (id.uuidString, privacy: .public)")
              } catch {
                  master.readabilityHandler = nil
                  try? master.close()
                  try? slave.close()
                  sink(.failed(sessionID: id, message: "Could not start the login shell: \(error.localizedDescription)"))
              }
          }
      
          private func received(_ data: Data, from session: Session) {
              let attention: TerminalAttentionKind? = lock.withLock {
                  guard sessions[session.id] === session else { return nil }
                  session.history.append(data)
                  if session.history.count > Self.maximumHistoryBytes {
                      session.history.removeFirst(session.history.count - Self.maximumHistoryBytes)
                  }
                  let text = String(decoding: data, as: UTF8.self)
                  session.promptTail = String((session.promptTail + text).suffix(1_024))
                  guard let kind = Self.detectAttention(in: session.promptTail) else { return nil }
                  if let last = session.lastAttention, last.kind == kind,
                     Date().timeIntervalSince(last.time) < 5 { return nil }
                  session.lastAttention = (kind, Date())
                  return kind
              }
      
              broadcast(.output(sessionID: session.id, data: data))
              if let attention {
                  let noun = attention == .password ? "a password" : "confirmation"
                  broadcast(.attention(
                      sessionID: session.id,
                      kind: attention,
                      message: "Terminal “\(session.name)” is waiting for \(noun)."
                  ))
              }
          }
      
          private func didExit(_ session: Session, status: Int32) {
              session.master.readabilityHandler = nil
              lock.withLock {
                  guard sessions[session.id] === session else { return }
                  session.isRunning = false
              }
              broadcast(.exited(sessionID: session.id, status: status))
              terminalLog.notice("terminal (session.id.uuidString, privacy: .public) exited with (status)")
          }
      
          private func close(id: UUID) {
              guard let session = lock.withLock({ sessions.removeValue(forKey: id) }) else { return }
              session.master.readabilityHandler = nil
              if session.isRunning { session.process.terminate() }
              try? session.master.close()
              broadcast(.closed(sessionID: id))
              terminalLog.notice("closed terminal (id.uuidString, privacy: .public)")
          }
      
          private func broadcast(_ event: TerminalEvent) {
              let current = lock.withLock { Array(sinks.values) }
              for sink in current { sink(event) }
          }
      
          private func normalizedName(_ requested: String) -> String {
              let trimmed = requested.trimmingCharacters(in: .whitespacesAndNewlines)
              let safe = String(trimmed.filter { character in
                  !character.isNewline && !character.unicodeScalars.contains {
                      CharacterSet.controlCharacters.contains($0)
                  }
              }.prefix(40))
              if !safe.isEmpty { return safe }
              return "Terminal \(lock.withLock { sessions.count + 1 })"
          }
      
          private func loginShell() -> String {
              if let record = getpwuid(getuid()), let shell = record.pointee.pw_shell {
                  let path = String(cString: shell)
                  if !path.isEmpty { return path }
              }
              return ProcessInfo.processInfo.environment["SHELL"] ?? "/bin/zsh"
          }
      
          private static func detectAttention(in rawText: String) -> TerminalAttentionKind? {
              let plain = rawText.replacingOccurrences(
                  of: "\\u{001B}\\[[0-?]*[ -/]*[@-~]",
                  with: "",
                  options: .regularExpression
              ).lowercased()
              let tail = String(plain.suffix(400))
              if tail.range(of: #"(?:password|passphrase)(?: for [^:\n]+)?:\s*$"#, options: .regularExpression) != nil {
                  return .password
              }
              let confirmationPatterns = [
                  #"(?:\[|\()(?:y/n|yes/no)(?:\]|\))\??\s*$"#,
                  #"are you sure[^\n]*\??\s*$"#,
                  #"press (?:enter|return)[^\n]*$"#,
                  #"continue\?\s*$"#,
              ]
              return confirmationPatterns.contains {
                  tail.range(of: $0, options: .regularExpression) != nil
              } ? .confirmation : nil
          }
      }
      
          // MARK: - Clipboard
          /// The most recently received host clipboard text. `nil` until the first pull.
          private(set) var hostClipboard: String?
          /// Whether a clipboard pull is in flight.
          private(set) var clipboardPulling = false
      
          // MARK: - Wake-on-LAN
          /// Hardware MAC addresses reported by the host's ServerHello. Stored by host ID.
          private(set) var hostMACAddresses: [String: [String]] = {
              guard let data = UserDefaults.standard.data(forKey: "hostMACAddresses"),
                    let decoded = try? JSONDecoder().decode([String: [String]].self, from: data) else {
                  return [:]
              }
              return decoded
          }()
      
          // MARK: - Audio
          private var audioPlayer: RemoteAudioPlayer?
      
          // MARK: - Terminals
          private(set) var terminals: [RemoteTerminal] = []
          private(set) var selectedTerminalID: UUID?
      
          /// What each trusted Mac is called, by the ID its pin is keyed by.
          ///
          /// The pin store knows only the ID, so the picker was listing `A1B2C3…` where the user
          /// expects the name of their Mac. The handshake is the only moment the client is told both,
          /// so it is the only moment this can be recorded.
          private(set) var hostNames: [String: String] = [:]
          /// The server ID of the currently (or most recently) connected Mac.
          private(set) var currentHostID: String?
      
          /// When each trusted Mac was last successfully connected to, keyed by host ID.
          private(set) var lastConnectedAt: [String: Date] = {
              guard let data = UserDefaults.standard.data(forKey: "hostLastConnectedAt"),
                    let decoded = try? JSONDecoder().decode([String: Date].self, from: data) else {
                  return [:]
              }
              return decoded
          }()
      
          private static let hostNamesKey = "Peering.hostNames"
      
          /// The failure to show the user, if the session is in one.
          ///
          /// The phase carries it and the diagnostics screen has always listed it, but the picker
          /// showed nothing at all — so a connection that failed instantly looked exactly like a
          /// button that did not work, which is a worse failure than the one being reported.
          var failureMessage: String? {
              if case .failed(let message) = phase { return message }
              case .video(let event):
                  apply(video: event)
              case .latency(let milliseconds):
                  latencyMilliseconds = milliseconds
              case .note(let message):
                  append(note: message)
              case .remoteNotice(let message):
                  remoteNotice = message
              case .clipboard(let text):
                  hostClipboard = text
                  clipboardPulling = false
              case .audio(let event):
                  switch event {
                  case .config(let config):
                      audioPlayer = RemoteAudioPlayer(config: config)
                  case .frame(let frame):
                      audioPlayer?.enqueue(frame)
                  }
              case .terminal(let event):
                  apply(terminal: event)
              }
          }
      
          private func apply(terminal event: TerminalEvent) {
              switch event {
              case .snapshot(let infos):
                  terminals = infos.map {
                      RemoteTerminal(id: $0.id, name: $0.name, isRunning: $0.isRunning)
                  }
                  if selectedTerminalID == nil || !terminals.contains(where: { $0.id == selectedTerminalID }) {
                      selectedTerminalID = terminals.first?.id
                  }
              case .created(let info):
                  terminals.removeAll { $0.id == info.id }
                  terminals.append(RemoteTerminal(id: info.id, name: info.name, isRunning: info.isRunning))
                  selectedTerminalID = info.id
                  append(note: "Terminal “\(info.name)” opened")
              case .output(let id, let data):
                  guard let index = terminals.firstIndex(where: { $0.id == id }) else { return }
                  terminals[index].output.append(data)
                  let limit = 512 * 1024
                  if terminals[index].output.count > limit {
                      terminals[index].output.removeFirst(terminals[index].output.count - limit)
                  }
              case .attention(let id, let kind, let message):
                  if let index = terminals.firstIndex(where: { $0.id == id }) {
                      terminals[index].attention = kind
                  }
                  selectedTerminalID = id
                  append(note: message)
                  notifier.terminalNeedsAttention(message, sessionID: id)
              case .exited(let id, let status):
                  if let index = terminals.firstIndex(where: { $0.id == id }) {
                      terminals[index].isRunning = false
                  }
                  append(note: "Terminal exited with status \(status)")
              case .closed(let id):
                  let name = terminals.first(where: { $0.id == id })?.name ?? "Terminal"
                  terminals.removeAll { $0.id == id }
                  if selectedTerminalID == id { selectedTerminalID = terminals.first?.id }
                  append(note: "\(name) closed")
              case .failed(_, let message):
                  append(note: "Terminal: \(message)")
              }
          }
      
          func refreshTerminals() { session?.sendTerminal(.list) }
      
          func createTerminal(name: String) {
              session?.sendTerminal(.create(name: name))
          }
      
          func selectTerminal(_ id: UUID) {
              selectedTerminalID = id
              if let index = terminals.firstIndex(where: { $0.id == id }) { terminals[index].attention = nil }
          }
      
          func sendTerminalInput(_ text: String, to id: UUID, appendReturn: Bool = true) {
              let suffix = appendReturn ? "\r" : ""
              guard let data = (text + suffix).data(using: .utf8) else { return }
              session?.sendTerminal(.input(sessionID: id, data: data))
              if let index = terminals.firstIndex(where: { $0.id == id }) { terminals[index].attention = nil }
          }
      
          func sendTerminalBytes(_ bytes: [UInt8], to id: UUID) {
              session?.sendTerminal(.input(sessionID: id, data: Data(bytes)))
          }
      
          func resizeTerminal(_ id: UUID, columns: Int, rows: Int) {
              session?.sendTerminal(.resize(
                  sessionID: id,
                  columns: UInt16(clamping: columns),
                  rows: UInt16(clamping: rows)
              ))
          }
      
          func closeTerminal(_ id: UUID) {
              session?.sendTerminal(.close(sessionID: id))
          }
      
          private func apply(video event: ClientSession.VideoEvent) {
              switch event {
              case .connecting:
                  videoStatus = "Opening the video channel…"
                  isVideoStreaming = false
              case .config(let config):
                  // The decoder half of this is done on the network queue, in the same place the
                  // frames are handled, so that it is ordered against them — see the event handler
                  // in `connect(to:)`. Flushing and reconfiguring here instead would put the reset
                  // *after* a keyframe that arrived in the same read, and throw that keyframe away.
                  // Only the labels belong on the main actor.

The second Mac visual pass is now substantially cooler: a larger live-session hero, ambient glass tint, double-bezel depth, an asymmetric devices/activity dashboard, richer connected-device tiles, and spring hover/press feedback.
